A probe pair message matches no admitted input (Rejection reasons, Counting model), so every rejection is counted per (socket, reason) on the receiving control socket `[decision]`. The rules run in the order of the table, the order of Admission messages, Validity rules: the magic and the version first, as Versioning requires; every drop is decided from the datagram's own bytes and the receiver's own module id in one pass, linear in the datagram's length (C-164), before the one row that needs the receiver's state; the first rule that applies decides the outcome, and a message of the same major and another minor is accepted (Versioning, Receiver behaviour) `[decision]`:

| Invalid form | Required outcome |
|---|---|
| too short: a datagram under 4 bytes, too small for the two leading fields every message starts with | drop and count under TooShort: dropped before any field is read `[decision]` |
| bad magic (the leading two bytes are not 0x4d4e) | drop and count under BadMagic |
| version mismatch (the major, the high byte of the version field, is not 1) | drop and count under VersionMismatch, and raise the VersionMismatch event (Versioning, Receiver behaviour) — never a silent drop; no reply is sent |
| a datagram of 4 to 31 bytes, too small for the common control header | drop and count under BadLength: the body does not fill the fixed size `[decision]` |
| domain id 0, the unconfigured domain (C-11) | ignore and count under UnconfiguredDomain — never ForeignDomain (C-173); no reply is sent |
| foreign domain: a non-zero domain id other than the local domain (C-11) | drop and count under ForeignDomain; no reply is sent |
| message type 34 or 35, which this section leaves unassigned, or a code the receiving module does not expect (Control message type codes) | drop and count under BadLength `[decision]` — provisional, as the Hello section rules; confirmed by 004-21 (Hello, Open items) |
| a datagram of 32 to 53 bytes, below the 54-byte fixed part of a probe or a reply | drop and count under BadLength: the body does not fill the fixed size `[decision]` |
| authentication scheme non-zero | drop and count under ReservedNonZero (Reserved fields, Authentication reservation (C-12)) |
| authentication tag non-zero | drop and count under ReservedNonZero (Reserved fields, Authentication reservation (C-12)) |
| recipient module id other than the receiving module's own | drop and count under BadLength `[decision]` — provisional: the message is not one the receiving module expects, as Admission messages rules; confirmed by 004-21 (Open item 4) |
| probe size different from its declared intended size: in a probe request, an intended packet size field below 54, or a datagram size other than that field | drop and count under BadLength: the body does not fill the size the field declares `[decision]` |
| a reply whose datagram size is other than its fixed 54 bytes | drop and count under BadLength: the body does not fill the fixed size, the shared rule of Rejection reasons and Admission messages, Validity rules `[decision]` |
| padding non-zero: in a probe request, any byte from offset 54 to the end of the datagram other than zero | drop and count under ReservedNonZero (Reserved fields, General rule for later tickets) `[decision]` |
| a probe request whose request id names no handshake the responder holds with its sender | not a parse rejection: the probe is well formed and is answered; the responder keeps no state (Matching a reply to its probe) `[decision]` |
| a reply that matches no outstanding probe: its sender, echoed request id or echoed size names no probe the receiving module has sent and not yet decided | not a parse rejection: the reply is well formed, and matching it needs the admission machine's per-handshake state; the admission machine decides (epic 005, 005-14, with 012-09) `[decision]` |

No value of a module id, the request id, the message counter or a reply's echoed intended packet size is a malformed case: each accepts every value its type holds, and only the rules above reject `[decision]`. The Rejection reasons enumeration is unchanged: every outcome above names an existing member whose Layers cell lists the control layer, or is not a rejection `[decision]`.

### Open items

**Open item 1 — attempts and wait.** No constant gives how many probes of one size are sent before that size counts as unanswered, or how long the source side waits for a reply; this section fixes only the sizes and the order of the sequence, and a single lost probe of each size refuses the connection if one probe per size is sent, as 012-09 sends. Owner: ticket 005-13 (admission handshake states and inputs), whose timers include the MTU probe silence, with 005-14 (admission handshake transition table), which writes the rows that act on it, and 012-09 (path MTU probe machine), which proposes the timeout as a pending-measurement parameter that L1 (task 019) confirms. Falsifier: probes on a healthy path go unanswered within the wait, or a connection on a healthy path is refused with PathMtuTooSmall because a probe was lost, at the loss rate the design expects `[decision]`.

**Open item 2 — what is recorded and where.** A reply proves only that the path carries at least the probed size, and the source side alone sees the replies; no admission message carries the outcome, because the landed accept is the consumer's and has no path MTU field (Admission messages, Admission accept), and the consumer does not learn from the pair alone which of its acknowledgements arrived. The path MTU of each connection belongs in the regime stamp (01-architecture.md § 3.3): 013-07 stamps a `path_mtu: u16` per connection, and 012-23 records it on both sides, but no ticket says what value is recorded — 012-09's `Confirmed(intended)` and `Fallback(1280)` are probe outcomes, not a measured path MTU, and 013-07's AC 4 example of 1 500 bytes is a value no probe sequence of this section establishes. No ticket of epic 004 lays out a path-MTU stamp section: 004-24 lays out the stamp container and request, 004-25 the section types the constants table names, none of them a path MTU, and 004-28 the observed DSCP and alignment group sections; further section types belong to the epics that stamp them (README of epic 004, gap R8). Owner: ticket 013-07 (network and connection sections), with 012-09 for the value its outcomes record. Falsifier: the recorded value must be the path's actual MTU rather than a bound the probes proved, or the consumer needs it before media arrives and cannot read it from the source side's regime stamp in time `[decision]`.

**Open item 3 — the probed path and the media path.** The probe goes to the address the source side sends its admission messages to, the consumer's control address, while media goes to the pinned destination address of the accept (Admission messages, Admission accept); for a multi-homed consumer the two may differ, so the path the probe proves may not be the media path. Owner: ticket 012-12 (admission engine, requester side), which sends the request and the first probe, and 012-14 (tick, probe timeout and pending re-check), which sends the probe at the fallback, with 005-14 (admission handshake transition table) for when the probe is sent relative to the accept, which is what would let it go to the pinned destination address. Falsifier: a multi-homed consumer's media path is smaller than the path the probe tested, so a connection admitted on the probe's reply drops media packets `[decision]`.

**Open item 4 — provisional BadLength outcomes and declared rates after a fallback.** An unused message type and a recipient module id that is not the receiver's are counted under BadLength provisionally, as in Admission messages; and the request's declared rates are computed for the intended packet size before the probe's outcome is known, so a connection that falls back to the multi-packet regime sends more packets per second than it declared (C-05; Admission messages, Declared rates). Owner: ticket 004-21 (validity rules completeness audit) for the two outcomes; epic 005, ticket 005-14, with 012-12 (admission engine, requester side) for the rates. Falsifier: 004-21 finds a member of the closed enumeration that names either case better; or a connection admitted on the fallback sends more packets or bit/s than its request declared without a re-admission `[decision]`.

**Open item 5 — which side refuses, against 012-13.** In this section the source side probes, sees the replies and refuses with PathMtuTooSmall, as the landed Admission messages, Who sends what, 012-12 and 012-14 have it; 012-13, the consumer side, lists PathMtuTooSmall among the consumer's checks, drives it in its AC 2 by dropping both the intended-size and the 1 280-byte probe, and returns a `path_mtu` in its `Accept`, while the consumer here only answers probes and does not learn which acknowledgements arrived. 012-13's AC 4, "a received MTU probe of size s is acknowledged with size s", is read here as an acknowledgement carrying the value s in its echoed intended packet size, not one of s bytes. Owner: ticket 012-13 (admission engine, consumer side), with 005-14 (admission handshake transition table), which fixes which side raises which reason, and 012-09 for the acknowledgement's size value. Falsifier: 012-13 AC 2 cannot be met as written under this section, since a consumer that answers probes cannot observe the silence that decides PathMtuTooSmall; or epic 005 places that refusal at the consumer; or 012-13 needs an acknowledgement of s bytes `[decision]`.

**Open item 6 — a sender that cannot set don't-fragment.** A probe sent without the DF bit can arrive in fragments and prove nothing, and the design does not say what a module does on a platform where it cannot set the bit: 001-04 and 001-09 test whether macOS and Windows can set it, and 021-03 sets it, but none states the behaviour when it cannot. Owner: no ticket owns it; the design owner, informed by epic 001's report on don't-fragment per OS (001-04, 001-09). Falsifier: a supported platform cannot set the DF bit on a UDP socket, so its probes, and its media, travel without it `[decision]`.

**Open item 7 — the reverse direction is never tested above 54 bytes.** By the design owner's decision the reply is a 54-byte acknowledgement, so the pair proves only the direction media flows; the reverse direction carries the receiver reports (up to 1 222 bytes), manifest sections and other control messages up to the 1 252-byte bound (C-17), and a reverse path smaller than that is found by no probe and refused with no PathMtuTooSmall, so the producer times out (C-41) instead. Owner: the design owner, with epic 005 (005-14) for the admission behaviour that follows. Falsifier: a path whose reverse direction carries less than 1 252 bytes of UDP payload admits a connection that then loses its receiver reports `[decision]`.

## Regime stamp

The regime stamp is a module's declaration of the configuration and health facts that peers and tools read to interpret its behaviour. C-18 sends it as a dedicated control message split into sections that each fit the control bound (C-17), multicast to the control group on any regime change and refreshed every 10 s, with a unicast request for an immediate fetch, and the hello carries only its version number (Hello, Regime stamp version number) `[decision]`. This section lays out the container — the stamp section and the stamp request, codes 36 and 37 of the block the Hello section reserves for 004-24 — fixes the maximum section count, the maximum stamp size and the storage a receiver needs, and states the sender rules, the receiver rules and every invalid form with its outcome `[decision]`. The section types, the bodies the container carries, are laid out by 004-25 (the types the constants table names) and 004-28 (observed DSCP and alignment group), and by later epics as minor changes, each epic that stamps state adding its own type (README of epic 004, gap R8) `[decision]`.

Behaviour the layouts serve, restated as fact: both messages are control messages, sent by UDP over IPv4 (C-100) at DSCP CS5 (40) (C-13), and both start with the common control header and its C-12 authentication reservation at the v1 values of C-115 — scheme 0, tag 128 zero bits, and a live message counter that is the sender's liveness counter, one counter per module instance shared by every control message it sends, stamp sections and stamp requests included (Hello, Liveness counter) `[decision]`. Sections travel multicast to the control group with TTL 1, and unicast in answer to a request; the request travels by unicast only `[decision]`. The hosted backend sends the stamp (epic 015, C-144) on the schedule of 013-24, and a device sends it through epic 026 (013-24); this section fixes the bytes and the rules any sender and receiver follow, not the sending code `[decision]`. Third parties read stream health only from the stamps, since receiver reports are not multicast and there is no query message (C-47): the stamp request fetches a module's whole stamp, never a part of it and never a health query `[decision]`.

The retired POC has no regime stamp message: the only stamp its gossip carries is the transport reference's claim stamp, a u64 on the `transport` line of the text hello (module.rs:153, `pub stamp: u64` in `struct Info`, and module.rs:273, the `transport` line of `Info::hello`) `[code]`. No field of the container carries a timeline epoch, so the container adds no carrier to Election messages, Which carrier binds whom `[decision]`. Two section types of 004-25 do carry one, per stream: the multi-packet regime section the epoch 004-08 hands over, and the injections section the epoch a stream is forced onto (Section types of the constants table); 004-25 adds both to Which carrier binds whom as binding no one's adoption `[decision]`. 006-19 asks for a 16-bit timeline epoch per record set in the stamp, the epoch of the line in force when the records were taken, and leaves the section that carries it to epic 013 (006-19, Work 3; C-196); neither 004-25 nor 004-28 lays out that per-record-set field, so adding its carrier to Which carrier binds whom is owned by epic 013, which places the field, together with the epic-004 ticket that lays out its section type in this section `[decision]`.

### Stamp section

A stamp section carries one part of one version of the sender's stamp. Little-endian (Byte order), packed with no padding (Alignment and padding); bytes 0 to 31 are the common control header (Hello, Common control header), bytes 32 to 57 the section header, which names the stamp, the section's place in it and the body's type, and the body follows `[decision]`:

| Offset (bytes) | Width (bytes) | Type | Meaning | Valid range |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 0 | 2 | u16 | Magic 0x4d4e, the ASCII letters M and N, fixed by 004-04 | 0x4d4e only |
| 2 | 2 | u16 | Protocol version, major in the high byte (bits 15..8), minor in the low byte; v1 packs 1.0 as 0x0100 (004-04) | major 1, any minor (Versioning, Receiver behaviour); senders write 0x0100 |
| 4 | 2 | u16 | Domain id (C-11); 0 is the unconfigured domain | 1 to 65535; 0 = unconfigured, ignored and counted (C-173) |
| 6 | 1 | u8 | Message type: 36 = regime stamp section (Control message type codes) | 36 only |
| 7 | 1 | u8 | Authentication scheme (C-12); 0 = none in v1 (C-115) | 0 only |
| 8 | 8 | u64 | Message counter (C-12): live (C-115), the sender's data-plane liveness counter (Hello, Liveness counter) | any 64-bit value |
| 16 | 16 | u128 | Authentication tag (C-12): 128 zero bits in v1 (C-115) | 0 only |
| 32 | 8 | u64 | Sender module id (C-11): the module whose stamp this section carries | any 64-bit value |
| 40 | 8 | u64 | Instance nonce (C-11) of the sender: with the module id, the instance whose stamp this is, as in the hello | any 64-bit value |
| 48 | 4 | u32 | Stamp version number (C-18): the version of the stamp this section belongs to, the hello's regime stamp version number | any 32-bit value |
| 52 | 1 | u8 | Section index: this section's place in the stamp, counted from 0 | 0 to section count − 1 |
| 53 | 1 | u8 | Section count: how many sections make up this stamp | 1 to 64 |
| 54 | 2 | u16 | Section type: the code of the body's layout (Section type codes) | 1 to 65535; codes not assigned in v1 are skipped by their length |
| 56 | 2 | u16 | Section length: how many body bytes follow | 0 to 1 194 |
| 58 | 1 × max 1194 | u8 | Body: the section's typed content, laid out by the table of its section type (004-25, 004-28, later epics) | section length bytes, at most 1 194 |

A stamp section's maximum size is 1 252 bytes: 32 + 8 + 8 + 4 + 1 + 1 + 2 + 2 = 58 bytes of header and at most 1 194 bytes of body, 58 + 1 194 = 1 252, exactly the control bound (C-17), as for the manifest section (Admission messages, Manifest section); a section with b body bytes is 58 + b bytes `[decision]`. The body follows the section length directly, and the section ends with the body's last byte `[decision]`.

The sender module id and the instance nonce name whose stamp a section belongs to, with the widths of the hello (C-11): the hello's staleness rule keys a copy by the pair (instance nonce, stamp version), so a section carries both, and a receiver matches it to a peer from its own bytes rather than from the datagram's source address, as a peer's hellos are matched by their module id and instance nonce `[decision]`. The stamp version number has the hello's width, 32 bits, and its two rules: a held copy is stale exactly when the pair (instance nonce, version number) of the peer's latest hello differs from the copy's, equality deciding within one nonce; and where an order is needed, versions of one instance nonce are ordered by serial comparison over half the range, v being newer than u exactly when (v − u) modulo 2^32 lies in 1 to 2^31 − 1, so a difference of 0 or 2^31 is never newer `[decision]`. This confirms Hello, Open item 6, whose owner is this ticket: its falsifier, a different width or comparison, does not hold `[decision]`.

A receiver holds a complete stamp of one version when, for one sender module id, instance nonce and stamp version, it holds a section of every index from 0 to the section count − 1, each stating the same section count; this is the only completeness test, and no total length is carried `[decision]`. The section length equals the datagram size less 58; it is carried because it is what a receiver checks the body against and what it skips an unknown body by, as for an announcement or a content message (Versioning, Minor and major changes) `[decision]`. A section type may occupy several sections of one stamp — 004-25 and 004-28 split a type over several sections where its entries do not fit one — and one stamp carries sections of many types; whether a type may appear in more than one section, and how its entries are divided between them, is fixed by that type's own layout `[decision]`.

### Section type codes

The section type is a u16 scoped to the regime stamp. Code 0 is never a section type, so a zeroed section header is never valid, and a block of codes is reserved for each epic-004 ticket that lays out section types, so tickets written in parallel cannot collide, as the Hello section reserves control message type codes `[decision]`:

| Codes | Section types | Assigned by |
|---|---|---|
| 0 | none: never a valid section type | this section |
| 1 to 15 | the section types the constants table names: multi-packet regime, per-connection health, calibration factor, commissioning window, injections, capability states, receive-timestamp source | 004-25 |
| 16 to 31 | observed DSCP of peers, alignment group | 004-28 |
| 32 to 65535 | unassigned in v1: the section types later epics add as minor changes, each assigned in this table by the ticket that lays its type out | a later ticket |

A code not assigned in v1 — any code from 32 to 65 535 today, and any code of the two blocks that 004-25 or 004-28 leaves unassigned — is an unknown section type: a receiver skips its body by its section length and keeps the section for the completeness test, never a rejection, because adding a section type is a minor change (Versioning, Minor and major changes; Reserved fields, Fields of the regime stamp) `[decision]`. Sixteen bits rather than eight because every epic that stamps state adds its own types for the whole life of the major version (gap R8), and the second byte costs one byte per section `[decision]`. A new layout for an existing section type is not a minor change: a section type's layout is fixed within a major version, so a changed layout takes a new code, and a receiver never meets two layouts under one code `[decision]`.

**Reported contradiction: independent stamp-format versioning.** Epic 013's definition of done carries the line "The stamp format is versioned independently of the media format, with the rule for unknown sections (skip and count)", and 013-02 states as a `[decision]` in its Context: "The stamp format is versioned independently of the media format." The merged wire specification gives every message one version: "Every message — media, control, sync and measurement — starts with the same two leading fields, a magic and a version" (Versioning), and the stamp section carries only that shared version in its common control header, with no stamp-format version of its own; the two statements contradict each other, and the contradiction is reported to the design owner, to whom the lead reported it on 2026-11-16 `[decision]`. Meanwhile this section follows the merged common control header: one protocol version versions the stamp as it versions every message, a new section type is a minor change skipped by its length, and a changed layout of a section type takes a new section type code `[decision]`.

The path MTU of each connection belongs in each side's regime stamp (Path-MTU probe pair, Exchange; 01-architecture.md § 3.3; 013-07), and no ticket of epic 004 lays out its section type, so this section does not add one `[decision]`. The container lets a later ticket add it as a minor change: its type takes a code from 32 to 65 535 in the table above, and a v1 receiver that does not know the code skips the section by its length `[decision]`.

### Stamp request

The stamp request asks one module for its whole current stamp at once (C-18). Little-endian (Byte order), packed with no padding (Alignment and padding); bytes 0 to 31 are the common control header (Hello, Common control header) `[decision]`:

| Offset (bytes) | Width (bytes) | Type | Meaning | Valid range |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 0 | 2 | u16 | Magic 0x4d4e, the ASCII letters M and N, fixed by 004-04 | 0x4d4e only |
| 2 | 2 | u16 | Protocol version, major in the high byte (bits 15..8), minor in the low byte; v1 packs 1.0 as 0x0100 (004-04) | major 1, any minor (Versioning, Receiver behaviour); senders write 0x0100 |
| 4 | 2 | u16 | Domain id (C-11); 0 is the unconfigured domain | 1 to 65535; 0 = unconfigured, ignored and counted (C-173) |
| 6 | 1 | u8 | Message type: 37 = regime stamp request (Control message type codes) | 37 only |
| 7 | 1 | u8 | Authentication scheme (C-12); 0 = none in v1 (C-115) | 0 only |
| 8 | 8 | u64 | Message counter (C-12): live (C-115), the sender's data-plane liveness counter (Hello, Liveness counter) | any 64-bit value |
| 16 | 16 | u128 | Authentication tag (C-12): 128 zero bits in v1 (C-115) | 0 only |
| 32 | 8 | u64 | Requested module id (C-11): the module whose stamp is requested | the receiving module's own module id |

The request's maximum size is 40 bytes, which is also its fixed size: 32 + 8 = 40, tiling bytes 0 to 39 with no gap, far within the control bound (C-17) `[decision]`. The requested module id is the only field the request needs: it names the module asked, at the hello's width (C-11), and a module that receives a request naming another module drops it (Validity rules) `[decision]`. The request carries no requester identity: the answer goes to the source address and port the request came from, the addressing a stateless answer needs, as the path-MTU probe reply is addressed (Path-MTU probe pair, Exchange) `[decision]`.

The request carries no stamp version `[decision]`. The answer is always the version the responder's hellos announce, the one it last multicast (Sender rules), because that is the only version a responder keeps; a version field would let a requester ask for a version the responder no longer holds, and a requester that already holds the announced version has no reason to ask, since by the hello's staleness rule its copy is then current `[decision]`.

How the stamp is returned `[decision]`: the responder answers a request by unicast, sending every section of its stamp to the requester alone, at the request's source address and port, and never answers by multicast to the control group. C-18 gives the unicast request an immediate fetch for the one module that asks, while a multicast answer would put up to 64 sections, 80 128 bytes, on the control socket of every module for one requester; at a cold start every module fetches the stamp of each of its up to 63 peers (C-217; 011-23 raises the fetch on each peer's first hello), so multicast answers would deliver each stamp to every module up to 63 times instead of once, and a module that missed nothing already holds the stamp from the multicast on change and the refresh every 10 s. This is also what 013-24 expects, the current stamp "for that requester only" `[decision]`.

### Maximum stamp size and per-peer storage

The maximum section count is 64 `[decision]`. The maximum stamp size is therefore 64 × 1 252 = 80 128 bytes on the wire, of which 64 × 1 194 = 76 416 bytes are body; the smallest stamp is one section of 58 bytes, with an empty body `[decision]`. The count is a fixed bound so that a receiver, a small device included, sizes its storage before it reads a section (Field-table format, rule 2; C-34) `[decision]`.

The bound of 64 is chosen, not derived from a constant: no constant bounds the connections one stamp reports (013-07), and the section types are laid out after this ticket (004-25, 004-28) `[decision]`. As a computed example, not a bound: 013-23 sketches a per-connection summary as eight u64 counters, two u32 counters, a dead flag and an optional observed DSCP, named by a connection identity of source module id, stream number, session and peer module id (8 + 2 + 4 + 8 = 22 bytes) with a producer or consumer role; encoded at those widths with one byte each for the flag, the role and the DSCP's presence, a summary is 8 × 8 + 2 × 4 + 1 + 2 + 22 + 1 = 98 bytes, one section holds ⌊1 194 / 98⌋ = 12 summaries, and the 256 summaries that 013-13's AC 3 builds take ⌈256 / 12⌉ = 22 sections, leaving 42 for every other section type `[decision]`. The widths are 004-25's to fix, and a stream with several consumers adds a summary per connection, so the example shows the bound is not tight at 013-13's test size, not that it suffices; 64 is kept as the chosen value `[decision]`. The section count is a u8, as the manifest section's is, and its valid range stops at 64 rather than 255 because the storage below grows with the bound: 255 sections would make every stored stamp up to 255 × 1 252 = 319 260 bytes; the bound's owner and falsifier are in Open item 1 `[decision]`.

A receiver needs at most 160 256 bytes of storage per peer: the last complete stamp it holds, at most 80 128 bytes, and one stamp being reassembled, at most 80 128 bytes more (Receiver rules) `[decision]`. Across the at most 63 peers of a module (C-217; at most 64 modules, C-34), a module that holds every peer's stamp needs at most 63 × 160 256 = 10 096 128 bytes of storage, and a sender keeps its own stamp as last multicast, at most 80 128 bytes, to answer requests `[decision]`. A module need not hold every peer's stamp, and which stamps a small device must hold is not a wire rule; Open item 2 records it with its owner and falsifier `[decision]`.

### Sender rules

The rules bind every module that sends a stamp, whoever sends it — the hosted backend (epic 015, C-144) on the schedule of 013-24, or a device through epic 026 `[decision]`:

1. **Multicast on change.** On any regime change the sender multicasts every section of its stamp, index 0 to the section count − 1, to the control group at once, under a new stamp version (C-18) `[decision]`.
2. **Refresh every 10 s.** Every 10 s the sender multicasts every section of its current stamp again, whether or not its regime changed (C-18) `[decision]`. Which change is a regime change and which only a counter change is epic 013's — its StampId covers regime fields only (013-05), and a counter change waits for the next refresh (013-24) — so counters that moved since the last send travel in the next refresh `[decision]`. 026a-11 has a device multicast "on every version change" (Work 3), and its AC 2 says "changing any stamp field bumps the version number in the next hello and triggers an immediate multicast"; once its stamp carries per-connection counters (026b-11), that is a multicast on every counter change, which this rule does not have — a present disagreement recorded in Open item 10 with its owner and falsifier `[decision]`.
3. **Version bump on any change.** The sender bumps the stamp version by one, modulo 2^32 (Hello, Regime stamp version number), each time it sends a stamp whose sections differ in any byte from offset 32 to the end, the stamp version field itself (bytes 48 to 51) excepted, from those of the version it last sent, a refresh with moved counters included; a refresh that differs in no such byte repeats the same version, identically `[decision]`. One version therefore names exactly one set of section bytes, which is what lets a receiver tell an identical repeat from a disagreement (Receiver rules); this is the ticket's "bump the version on any change" and 026a-11's "Bump the stamp version number on any field change" read literally `[decision]`. Three consequences follow `[decision]`. First, an identical repeat occurs only while nothing in the stamp has changed: the refresh of an idle stamp, or an answer to a request that duplicates a multicast already received `[decision]`. Second, a change of version does not signal a regime change: a refresh with moved counters changes the version too, and 013-05 says "Counters are refreshed every 10 s (C-18) and are not a regime change"; what marks a regime change is epic 013's StampId, a function of the regime fields only (013-05), which this section neither defines nor carries `[decision]`. Third, a version change announced by a hello is not by itself a reason to fetch, because the multicast delivers each new version before any hello names it (rule 4); a receiver fetches only under receiver rule 9 `[decision]`.
4. **The hello names the version last multicast.** The stamp version number of the sender's hellos is the version of the stamp it last multicast, never a version not yet sent, so no hello sends a peer to fetch a stamp that does not exist (Hello, Regime stamp version number) `[decision]`.
5. **Unicast fetch.** On a valid request naming its own module id, the sender at once sends every section of the version it last multicast by unicast to the request's source address and port (Stamp request); an answer never creates a version, so an answered section and its multicast copy are identical from offset 32 to the end, as 026a-11 asks of a device (AC 3: "unicast, with sections equal to the latest multicast") `[decision]`.
6. **Liveness counter.** Every section and every request carries the sender's liveness counter in its message counter, shared with its hellos and every other control message (Hello, Liveness counter), so two sends of one section differ in bytes 8 to 15 when the data plane has progressed between them; this is why sections are compared from offset 32 `[decision]`.
7. **Bounds and order.** Every section is at most 1 252 bytes (C-17) and a stamp at most 64 sections, and a stamp that needs more cannot be sent (Open item 1, with its owner and falsifier); sections are sent in index order, and a receiver accepts them in any order `[decision]`.

### Receiver rules

A receiver keeps, per peer module id, at most two stamps — the last complete stamp it holds and at most one stamp being reassembled — and matches a section to a stamp by its sender module id, instance nonce and stamp version `[decision]`. A section that passes the validity rules is handled by the first of rules 1 to 7 below that applies; rules 8 to 10 are not alternatives to them and apply alongside, rule 8 to every section whatever rule 1 to 7 handles it, rules 9 and 10 to the stamps a receiver holds `[decision]`:

1. **Whose stamp.** A section whose sender module id has no entry in the receiver's discovery map, whose module id the discovery map holds in quarantine after a collision (C-46; 005-06: third parties "ignore both"), or whose instance nonce is not the nonce of that peer's latest hello, is ignored: not a rejection, because the pair is judged against the discovery map's per-peer state, which no packet rule holds, and the hello's staleness rule keys a copy by that nonce (Hello, Regime stamp version number; 005-06, 005-07) `[decision]`. A restarted module's first stamp may therefore be ignored until its first hello, and the receiver then fetches it (rule 9) `[decision]`.
2. **A new instance.** A section whose instance nonce, the nonce of the peer's latest hello by rule 1, differs from the nonce of the held stamp or of the stamp being reassembled discards both, the old instance's complete and partial stamp alike, and starts reassembling the section's version; versions of different instance nonces are never compared, because the Hello orders versions only within one nonce and a new instance may start its count at any value (Hello, Regime stamp version number) `[decision]`.
3. **The first stamp.** When nothing is held and nothing is being reassembled for the peer, the first section received starts the reassembly of its version, whatever the version `[decision]`.
4. **The held version.** A section of the version of the held complete stamp is compared with the held section of the same index: an identical section, equal from offset 32 to the end, is ignored and the held section kept; a section that disagrees is a rejection (Validity rules) `[decision]`.
5. **The version being reassembled.** A section of the version being reassembled is compared the same way when its index is already held, and stored when it is not; once every index from 0 to the section count − 1 is held, the stamp is complete and replaces the held stamp `[decision]`.
6. **A newer version.** A section of a version newer than the one being reassembled, or than the held stamp when none is being reassembled, by the hello's serial comparison within one instance nonce, discards the incomplete stamp being reassembled, if any, and starts reassembling the newer version with this section; the held complete stamp is kept until the newer one completes `[decision]`.
7. **An older version.** A section of any other version of the same instance nonce — older than the newest the receiver holds or reassembles, or at a distance of 2^31 from it — is ignored: it is a late or reordered copy, not a rejection `[decision]`.
8. **Unknown section type.** A section whose type the receiver does not know, of the same major version, is skipped by its section length: the receiver keeps the section for the completeness test and the repeat comparison and does not parse its body (Versioning, Minor and major changes; 004-04) `[decision]`. A receiver may count the sections it skips, as 013-02 asks ("skips it and counts it"); that count is a local diagnostic, neither a wire field nor a member of the Rejection reasons enumeration `[decision]`.
9. **Fetch.** A receiver fetches a peer's stamp by unicast request only when its held copy is stale, that is when the pair (instance nonce, stamp version) of the peer's latest hello differs from the pair of its held stamp (Hello, Regime stamp version number), and the sections of that pair have not completed by multicast; this rule binds every reader of the stamp, and a change of version announced by a hello is not by itself a reason to fetch `[decision]`. The multicast delivers each new version before any hello names it (sender rule 4), so a receiver that received it holds the hello's pair and does not fetch; it fetches when it has no stamp of the peer (a peer's first hello), when the peer restarted, or when sections of the announced version were lost `[decision]`. How long a receiver waits for a reassembly of the announced version to complete before it fetches, and how often it asks again, are fixed by no constant, and this section invents no figure (Open item 6, with its owner and falsifier) `[decision]`.
10. **What the receiver uses meanwhile.** While a newer version is incomplete, the receiver's stamp of that peer is the held complete one, which the hello's rule then calls stale; it never uses an incomplete stamp and never mixes sections of two versions or of two instances, and whether a reader may act on a stale copy is not decided here (Open item 5, with its owner and falsifier) `[decision]`.

A request that passes the validity rules is answered under sender rule 5; the responder keeps no state per requester `[decision]`.

### Validity rules

Every malformed section or request is dropped — or ignored, for the unconfigured domain — and counted under one member of the closed Rejection reasons enumeration; nothing is dropped silently, and a dropped request is not answered `[decision]`. A stamp section or request matches no admitted input (Rejection reasons, Counting model), so every rejection is counted per (socket, reason) on the receiving control socket `[decision]`. The rules run in the order of the table, the order of Admission messages, Validity rules: the magic and the version first, as Versioning requires; every row up to the section length is decided from the datagram's own bytes and the receiver's own module id in one pass, linear in the datagram's length (C-164); the last three rows are reassembly rules, decided against the sections already held for the same sender module id, instance nonce and stamp version, as Manifest, Validity rules decides its own; the first rule that applies decides the outcome, and a message of the same major and another minor is accepted (Versioning, Receiver behaviour) `[decision]`:

| Invalid form | Required outcome |
|---|---|
| too short: a datagram under 4 bytes, too small for the two leading fields every message starts with | drop and count under TooShort: dropped before any field is read `[decision]` |
| bad magic (the leading two bytes are not 0x4d4e) | drop and count under BadMagic |
| version mismatch (the major, the high byte of the version field, is not 1) | drop and count under VersionMismatch, and raise the VersionMismatch event (Versioning, Receiver behaviour) — never a silent drop; no answer is sent |
| a datagram of 4 to 31 bytes, too small for the common control header | drop and count under BadLength: the body does not fill the fixed size `[decision]` |
| domain id 0, the unconfigured domain (C-11) | ignore and count under UnconfiguredDomain — never ForeignDomain (C-173); no answer is sent |
| foreign domain: a non-zero domain id other than the local domain (C-11) | drop and count under ForeignDomain; no answer is sent |
| message type 0, a code in 38 to 47 that this section leaves unassigned, or a code the receiving module does not expect (Control message type codes) | drop and count under BadLength `[decision]` — provisional, as the Hello section rules (Hello, Validity rules); confirmed by 004-21 |
| oversize: a datagram larger than 1 252 bytes (C-17) | drop and count under OversizeControl |
| datagram size below the fixed part of its message type — 58 bytes for a section — or other than the fixed 40 bytes of a request | drop and count under BadLength: the body does not fill the fixed size `[decision]` |
| authentication scheme non-zero | drop and count under ReservedNonZero (Reserved fields, Authentication reservation (C-12)) |
| authentication tag non-zero | drop and count under ReservedNonZero (Reserved fields, Authentication reservation (C-12)) |
| requested module id other than the receiving module's own, in a request | drop and count under BadLength `[decision]` — provisional: the message is not one the receiving module expects, as Admission messages rules for a recipient module id; confirmed by 004-21 |
| section count of 0, or above 64, its fixed maximum | drop and count under BadLength: a count outside its fixed range `[decision]` |
| section index not below the section count | drop and count under BadLength: the index lies outside the stamp its section declares `[decision]` |
| section type 0 | drop and count under BadLength `[decision]` — provisional: no section type has code 0 (Section type codes); confirmed by 004-21 |
| section length overrun: a section length above 1 194, or a datagram size other than 58 + the section length | drop and count under BadLength: the body does not fill the size the length declares `[decision]` |
| sections of one stamp version disagree on the section count: a section states a section count different from that of a section already held for the same sender module id, instance nonce and stamp version | drop the datagram and every section held for that stamp version, the held complete stamp included, and count once under BadLength: the sections do not form one declared shape `[decision]` |
| duplicate section that disagrees: a section of one stamp version whose index is already held for the same sender module id and instance nonce, with a different section type, section length or body | drop the datagram and every section held for that stamp version, the held complete stamp included, and count once under BadLength: the sections do not form one declared shape `[decision]` |
| identical repeat: a section whose sender module id, instance nonce, stamp version and section index are those of a section already held, equal to it from offset 32 to the end | not a rejection: the repeat is ignored and counted under no Reject reason, and the section already held is kept, as merged text treats every duplicate as valid (below) `[decision]` |

The identical repeat, the last row, is not a rejection: it is ignored and counted under no Reject reason, and the section already held is kept, as merged text treats every duplicate as valid: a duplicated receiver report is "a valid message, never a rejection" (Receiver report, Report number), a duplicate sync reply "a valid exchange" (Sync request and reply, Exchange rules), and of media copies "the first valid copy wins" (media rule 9; Reserved fields, Copy index (C-07)) `[decision]`. The refresh every 10 s (C-18) repeats every section of an unchanged stamp, so a rejection here would count every refresh of every peer `[decision]`.

Not a rejection either: a section of an unknown section type, skipped by its length; a section of an older version, or of a module or instance nonce the discovery map does not hold (Receiver rules) `[decision]`. No value of a module id, an instance nonce, a stamp version, the message counter or a body byte of an unknown type is a malformed case: each accepts every value its type holds, and only the rules above reject `[decision]`. The validity of a known type's body is that type's own rules (004-25, 004-28), applied once the section passes the rules above; a section dropped under them leaves its stamp incomplete until the next refresh or fetch `[decision]`. The Rejection reasons enumeration is unchanged: every outcome above except the identical repeat names an existing member whose Layers cell lists the control layer `[decision]`.

### Machine-readable tables

wire.toml declares the stamp section as `regime stamp section`, with `section_of = "regime stamp"`, the 004-02 attribute, and the stamp request as `regime stamp request`, without it `[decision]`. The parent `regime stamp` is named, not declared as a message: the stamp is not a datagram but the set of sections that share one sender module id, instance nonce, stamp version and section count, up to 80 128 bytes, and the schema can declare no control message above 1 252 bytes and has no notion of a payload joined from several messages — the tooling gap the Manifest section records, owned by ticket 004-23, to which this case is added `[decision]`. Codes 38 to 47, the rest of the block the Hello section reserves for 004-24, are unused in v1 `[decision]`.

### Section types of the constants table

This part lays out the seven section types the constants table names, codes 1 to 7 of the block 1 to 15 that Section type codes gives 004-25, in the order of the ticket: multi-packet regime (1; C-05), per-connection health (2; C-47, C-159), calibration factor (3; C-106, C-176), commissioning window (4; C-107, C-153), injections (5; C-120, C-145), capability states (6; C-127, C-128, C-129, C-147, C-161) and receive-timestamp source (7; C-131) `[decision]`. Codes 8 to 15 stay unassigned in v1, so a v1 receiver skips a section of one of them by its length (Section type codes), and the block 16 to 31 of 004-28 is untouched `[decision]`.

Every table below is body-relative: its offsets are byte distances from the section's first body byte, byte 58 of the section (Stamp section), little-endian (Byte order) and packed with no padding (Alignment and padding), as the manifest's tables count from the manifest's first byte (Manifest) `[decision]`. A section whose body is b bytes is 58 + b bytes and states b as its section length; sub-byte fields are written bits [hi:lo] of a named byte, as Bit-field numbering fixes `[decision]`.

A list type's body is a one-byte entry count followed by that many entries of the type's fixed entry size `[decision]`. A list type whose entries do not fit one section is split over several sections of the same type code, each a complete body with its own entry count; read in section index order, the entries of all the type's sections are in the type's key order with each key once, so a section's first entry follows the last entry of the type's section with the next lower index `[decision]`. Sorting makes the bytes of an unchanged state identical from one send to the next, which sender rule 3 needs to repeat a version, and lets a receiver find a repeated key by comparing neighbours (Validity rules of the section types) `[decision]`.

Which of these types a stamp carries `[decision]`:

- **Multi-packet regime:** only while at least one stream of the sender is in the multi-packet regime; a stream the section does not list uses the default regime, one packet per slice (Slice header, Multi-packet regime) `[decision]`.
- **Per-connection health:** while the sender has at least one admitted connection, as producer or as consumer `[decision]`.
- **Commissioning window:** exactly while a window is open, and never otherwise: C-153 has modules stamp it "while open", so the absence of the section is the closed state and there is no closed marker `[decision]`.
- **Calibration factor, injections, capability states, receive-timestamp source:** in every stamp, one section each; the injections section is sent with an entry count of 0 when no injection is active, as 013-09 asks (Work 3: an empty list means "none active" and is still written) `[decision]`.

Every counter these types carry — the eleven health counters of each connection and the use counts of the injection hooks — changes its section's bytes when it moves, so under sender rule 3 (Sender rules) a refresh with moved counters is sent under a new stamp version, as is a change of any other field of these types `[decision]`. Readers detect a regime change by the StampId of epic 013 (013-05), a function of the regime fields only, never by the stamp version; which fields of these types are regime fields and which are counters is epic 013's to say (013-05, 013-23), and this layout neither decides it nor carries it `[decision]`.

Two of these types carry a timeline epoch at the C-10 width of 16 bits: the multi-packet regime section the epoch a stream's regime is stamped for, which 004-08 hands over (Slice header, Multi-packet regime), and the injections section the epoch a stream is forced onto (C-120) `[decision]`. Both state what the sender does, no module adopts an epoch from a stamp, and both are carriers that bind no one's adoption in Election messages, Which carrier binds whom `[decision]`.

wire.toml declares each type as a message of its own with `section_of = "regime stamp"`: the section header of Stamp section with the type's code as its only section type, followed by the type's body from bit 464; each list entry is a counted field of type `uint` whose own layout is a message without `section_of`, as the receiver report member is declared (Receiver report, Per-member part) `[decision]`.

### Multi-packet regime section

Section type code 1 `[decision]`. The section declares, per stream of the sender in the multi-packet regime (C-05), the values 004-08 hands over: the stream number, the packets per slice, the frames per slice and the timeline epoch the stamped positions are counted in (Slice header, Multi-packet regime) `[decision]`:

| Offset (bytes) | Width (bytes) | Type | Meaning | Valid range |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 0 | 1 | u8 | Entry count: how many stream entries follow in this section | 1 to 149 |
| 1 | 8 × max 149 | byte blob of stream entries | Stream entry: one stream of the sender in the multi-packet regime, laid out as the entry table below; entries in ascending stream number | entry count entries, at most 149 (1 192 bytes) |

The stream entry is 8 bytes; offsets are byte distances from the entry's first byte `[decision]`:

| Offset (bytes) | Width (bytes) | Type | Meaning | Valid range |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 0 | 2 | u16 | Stream number (C-10) of the stream at this source, the sender; the key, each stream at most once in the stamp | 0 to 255 (C-09) |
| 2 | 2 | u16 | Packets per slice: the packet count every packet of the stream's slices carries in its slice header (Slice header) | 2 to 65 535 |
| 4 | 2 | u16 | Frames per slice: the stream's slice length, the frames field of its slices, which steps its position grid (Slice header) | 1 to 65 535 |
| 6 | 2 | u16 | Timeline epoch (C-10): the epoch the stream's packet headers carry while this regime is stamped, the epoch its positions are counted in (Slice header, Multi-packet regime) | any 16-bit value |

Sizes `[decision]`: 149 × 8 = 1 192 bytes of entries, a body of 1 + 1 192 = 1 193 bytes, and the maximum section size is 58 + 1 193 = 1 251 bytes, within the control bound of 1 252 bytes (C-17). At most 149 entries per section, because ⌊1 193 / 8⌋ = 149 and the count is one byte `[decision]`. A source has at most 256 streams (C-09), so at its maxima the type needs ⌈256 / 149⌉ = 2 sections `[decision]`.

The packet count is 2 or more because a stream with one packet per slice is in the default regime and is not listed (C-05); the widths are the slice header's, so each value is the one its packets carry (Slice header, Field table) `[decision]`. **Frames per slice in two places, decided here.** The frames per slice is also in the stream's manifest element, and for an admitted connection the manifest's value is authoritative: it is the slice length that admission checked the declared slice rate against (Admission messages, Declared rates; SliceRate, C-185) and the length the receiver report's slice counters step by (Manifest, Stream element) `[decision]`. The multi-packet entry restates that value and equals it; a stream's frames per slice does not change while a connection of it is admitted, because a change would alter the slices per second that admission accepted without a new check, so a source changes it only through a re-admission with a new manifest `[decision]`. A reader that finds the two different uses the manifest's value for the connection and treats the stamp entry as a fault of the sender; this is not a parse rejection, because deciding it needs the connection's admitted manifest, which no stamp rule holds `[decision]`. This decides the question of Manifest, Open item 9, and with it Size budget, Open item (frames-per-slice changes): a stream's frames per slice never changes mid-stream within one admitted connection, so no change of s adds a break while an announcement is pending `[decision]`. Both items name this ticket as their owner, and neither stays open: the Manifest item's falsifier, a stamp and a manifest stating different frames per slice for one stream, is now a sender fault with the reader's outcome above, and the Size budget item's falsifier, frames per slice changing mid-stream, cannot occur within one admitted connection `[decision]`.

The timeline epoch is the fourth value 004-08's merged text hands over ("the timeline epoch the stamped positions are counted in"), one more than its ticket's Work 3 lists, and this section carries it: a consumer reads which epoch a stamped regime was declared for, while the packet header's epoch says which epoch a packet is on (Media packet header) `[decision]`. On an epoch change the source's session changes too (C-19), and the entry changes with it `[decision]`.

### Per-connection health section

Section type code 2 `[decision]`. Producer and consumer each publish here, per connection, the eleven counters of C-159 in its order and the observed DSCP of C-47: third parties read stream health only from the stamps, since receiver reports are not multicast and there is no query message (C-47) `[decision]`. A connection is one consumer module's admission to one bundle of a source (Admission messages, One admitted connection per consumer and bundle), and the receiver report counts per member of it (Receiver report, Per-member part); this section does the same, one member entry per member of each connection, so an entry is one stream of one connection `[decision]`:

| Offset (bytes) | Width (bytes) | Type | Meaning | Valid range |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 0 | 1 | u8 | Entry count: how many member entries follow in this section | 1 to 18 |
| 1 | 65 × max 18 | byte blob of member entries | Member entry: one member stream of one connection of the sender, as producer or consumer, laid out as the entry table below; entries in key order | entry count entries, at most 18 (1 170 bytes) |

The member entry is 65 bytes: 19 that name the member and its admission, eleven 32-bit counters and 2 bytes of observed DSCP; offsets are byte distances from the entry's first byte `[decision]`:

| Offset (bytes) | Width (bytes) | Type | Meaning | Valid range |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 0 | 8 | u64 | Peer module id (C-11): the consumer's module id in a producer entry, the source's module id in a consumer entry | any 64-bit value |
| 8 | 2 | u16 | Stream number (C-10) of the member stream at its source | 0 to 255 (C-09) |
| 10 | 4 | u32 | Session (C-10) of the stream, as its source assigns it and every consumer sees it (C-19) | any 32-bit value |
| 14 | 4 | u32 | Admission request id: the request id of the admission handshake that admitted this connection (Admission messages, Common admission fields), the same on both sides; a re-admission carries a new one | any 32-bit value |
| 18 | 1 bit | uint | Role, bit [0:0] of byte 18: 0 = producer, the sender sends the stream; 1 = consumer, the sender consumes it | 0 or 1 |
| 18 | 7 bits | uint | Reserved, bits [7:1] of byte 18 (Reserved fields, General rule for later tickets) | 0 only |
| 19 | 4 | u32 | Received: slices accepted with content, neither late, missing nor a marked gap; cumulative, wraps modulo 2^32 | any 32-bit value (wrap rule below) |
| 23 | 4 | u32 | Late: slices counted missing at their deadline that arrived later while their position was within the ring span, reclassified late and discarded (C-108); cumulative, wraps modulo 2^32 | any 32-bit value (wrap rule below) |
| 27 | 4 | u32 | Missing: slices absent at their deadline and not reclassified late since; decreases by one on every reclassification (C-108); wraps modulo 2^32 | any 32-bit value (wrap rule below) |
| 31 | 4 | u32 | Marked gap: positions the producer marked with a gap slice (rule 10), counted in slices; cumulative, wraps modulo 2^32 | any 32-bit value (wrap rule below) |
| 35 | 4 | u32 | Other-epoch: packets on another timeline epoch, never a miss, each restarting the dead clock (C-135, C-102, C-111); cumulative, wraps modulo 2^32 | any 32-bit value (wrap rule below) |
| 39 | 4 | u32 | Too-early: packets that arrived too early for the consumer to place their slice and were discarded; cumulative, wraps modulo 2^32 | any 32-bit value (wrap rule below) |
| 43 | 4 | u32 | Duplicates: packets discarded as duplicates by stream, position and packet index, the first valid copy winning (rule 9); cumulative, wraps modulo 2^32 | any 32-bit value (wrap rule below) |
| 47 | 4 | u32 | Incomplete reassemblies: multi-packet slices not complete at their reassembly deadline (C-15, C-110), counted in slices; cumulative, wraps modulo 2^32 | any 32-bit value (wrap rule below) |
| 51 | 4 | u32 | Demoted rounds: rounds of the consumer, one per slice period, in which the member's input was Demoted and treated as gapped (C-109); cumulative, wraps modulo 2^32 | any 32-bit value (wrap rule below) |
| 55 | 4 | u32 | Interrupted events: entries into Interrupted after 8 consecutive missed slices (C-38), only missing or late slices counting as misses (C-102); events, not slices; cumulative, wraps modulo 2^32 | any 32-bit value (wrap rule below) |
| 59 | 4 | u32 | Dead: entries into Dead, 2 s without data counted from the last slice with content or a marked gap (C-39, C-111); events, not slices; cumulative, wraps modulo 2^32 | any 32-bit value (wrap rule below) |
| 63 | 1 | u8 | Observed DSCP state: the C-129 capability state of the observed DSCP of this member (Capability states section) | 1 to 4 |
| 64 | 6 bits | uint | Observed DSCP (C-47), bits [5:0] of byte 64: the DSCP of the IPv4 header of the last packet of this member stream that reached the consumer; EF (46) is what the producer sends (C-13) | 0 to 63; 0 unless the state is present (1) |
| 64 | 2 bits | uint | Reserved, bits [7:6] of byte 64 (Reserved fields, General rule for later tickets) | 0 only |

Sizes `[decision]`: 18 × 65 = 1 170 bytes of entries, a body of 1 + 1 170 = 1 171 bytes, and the maximum section size is 58 + 1 171 = 1 229 bytes, within the control bound (C-17). At most 18 entries per section, because ⌊1 193 / 65⌋ = 18 `[decision]`. How many member entries one module has, from the constants `[decision]`: as producer, each of its at most 256 streams (C-09) is a member of at most one connection per consumer module, because a consumer module holds at most one admitted connection per bundle of a source (Admission messages, One admitted connection per consumer and bundle) and a stream belongs to one bundle (Receiver report, Bundle identity and member rows), and the consumers are among the module's at most 63 peers (C-217), so 256 × 63 = 16 128 producer entries; as consumer, by the same keys, at most 63 × 256 = 16 128 consumer entries. The type's total maximum is 2 × 16 128 = 32 256 member entries, both directions of the 256 streams of C-09 and the 63 peers of C-217, and at its maxima it needs ⌈32 256 / 18⌉ = 1 792 sections, beyond the bound of 64 sections (Section count at the maxima) `[decision]`.

An entry names its member by its peer module id, its stream number, its admission request id and its role: the stream's source is the sender in a producer entry and the peer in a consumer entry, so with the section's sender module id the entry names the source's module id, the stream number and the consumer's module id, the keys of a receiver report's connection and row (Receiver report, Bundle identity and member rows) `[decision]`. The session tells a stream restarted, or moved to another timeline epoch, from its earlier self (C-19), and the admission request id tells one admission of the connection from the next: the source side does not reuse a request id while a handshake or an admitted connection under it exists (Admission messages, Common admission fields), so a re-admission, which keeps the key and the session, carries a new request id `[decision]`. The key order is role, then peer module id, then stream number, then admission request id, each ascending; a key appears once in the stamp, so an ended connection that the sender still lists (012-23 keeps a terminal slot listed until it is reused) and its re-admission are two entries `[decision]`.

**Wrap rule.** Every counter is 32 bits, starts at 0 when the connection is admitted, is cumulative, and wraps modulo 2^32, as every counter of the receiver report wraps; none saturates `[decision]`. A reader takes the change between two stamps as the difference modulo 2^32, read as a signed value from −2^31 to 2^31 − 1, the receiver report's rule (Receiver report, Counters): that keeps the C-108 reclassification readable, missing falling by one when late grows by one, and keeps a wrapped counter readable, while a saturated counter would stop telling changes and could not show a decrease of missing `[decision]`. A reader computes that difference only between two entries with the same key, admission request id included: an entry with a new request id is a new admission whose counters restarted at 0, a reset the reader takes as a new baseline and never as a wrap or a negative change `[decision]`. A producer entry carries the counters its consumer reports, built from the changes between reports (Receiver report, Counters); a consumer entry carries the consumer's own counts `[decision]`.

The width is 32 bits for all eleven, not the report's 16 for five of them, because a stamp is read once per refresh of 10 s (C-18), not every 100 ms (C-40): in 10 s a slice counter changes by at most 96 000 × 10 = 960 000 slices, at 96 000 slices per second (Receiver report, Counters), and a packet counter by less than 1 488 000 000 packets at 100 Gbit/s, five times the report's 297.6 million in 2 s, whose frame size is a figure the report recalls from IEEE Std 802.3 `[memory]`. Both stay inside the 2 147 483 647 that a 32-bit signed difference resolves, so a reader that takes two consecutive refreshes reads every change unambiguously; one that misses refreshes for more than about 14 s may misread a packet counter at that line rate, and a slice counter only after more than 2 147 483 647 / 96 000 s, about 22 369 s `[decision]`. 013-23's sketch of about 98 bytes per summary uses eight u64 counters, and the merged text leaves the wire widths to 004-25 (Maximum stamp size, the computed example); an entry is 98 − 65 = 33 bytes smaller than that sketch, and with eleven 64-bit counters it would be 109 bytes, ⌊1 193 / 109⌋ = 10 entries per section against 18 `[decision]`.

**Dead and demoted.** Dead is a count of entries into Dead, events like interrupted events, not a flag: C-159 names it among the per-connection counters, and the receiver report counts entries into Dead (Receiver report, Interrupted, demoted, dead) `[decision]`. A reader that needs to know whether an input is dead now reads the Dead alarm of the sender's hello (Hello, Alarm field; C-173), which says that one of its inputs is, and the entry whose dead count grew says which; 013-23 sketches dead as a flag, and that difference is Open item 14, with its owner and falsifier `[decision]`. The demoted-rounds counter makes the Demoted state of C-109 visible to third parties: it grows only while the input is Demoted, whereas a gapped input grows marked gap or missing (Receiver report, Interrupted, demoted, dead) `[decision]`.

**Observed DSCP.** The observed DSCP is stamped with one of the four capability states of C-129, as the receiver report hands over ("C-129's absent or unverified state, as 004-25 stamps it") and 026b-11 asks `[decision]`. Present (1): the DSCP holds a value read from a received packet, in a consumer entry the consumer's own reading and in a producer entry the value its consumer reported with the report's Observed bit at 1 `[decision]`. Absent (2): the consumer cannot read the DSCP of received packets, written in a consumer entry only `[decision]`. Unverified (3): no packet has been read yet, or, in a producer entry, the consumer reported the Observed bit at 0, which does not say which of absent or not yet `[decision]`. Not requested (4): a configuration that deliberately turns the observation off; no landed configuration does, so a v1 sender does not write it, and a receiver accepts it as a member of the closed enumeration `[decision]`. In every state but present the DSCP bits are 0 `[decision]`.

### Calibration factor section

Section type code 3 `[decision]`. The section carries the calibration factor of the sender's target (C-106): the time per counted abstract operation, measured once per listed target by the DSL conformance suite of epic 025 running a reference workload (C-176), with which admission converts the manifest's cost bound (Manifest, Manifest layout) `[decision]`:

| Offset (bytes) | Width (bytes) | Type | Meaning | Valid range |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 0 | 8 | u64 | Calibration factor (C-106, C-176): time per counted abstract operation on the sender's target, in femtoseconds per operation | 1 to 18 446 744 073 709 551 614 fs per operation; 0xffffffffffffffff = not calibrated, the target has no recorded factor; 0 is not valid |

The maximum section size, which is also its fixed size, is 58 + 8 = 66 bytes `[decision]`. The factor is one value per target (C-176), and a module runs on one target, so the section carries one value and every stamp carries the section once `[decision]`.

The unit agrees with the manifest's: the cost bound is a u64 of counted abstract operations per slice (C-106), so the cost bound times the factor is femtoseconds per slice, the time admission compares with what is left of its slice period, and the product of two u64 values fits 128 bits, so admission computes it exactly in integer arithmetic `[decision]`. Femtoseconds in a fixed-point integer, for three reasons `[decision]`: no constant bounds the time of one operation, and a target that runs one in under a nanosecond would have a factor of 0 in whole nanoseconds; 64 bits of femtoseconds reach about 18 446 s per operation, so no factor overflows; and an integer has one encoding per value, so an unchanged factor gives unchanged bytes, which sender rule 3 compares, where a float has two zeros and many NaN encodings `[decision]`. 012-03 holds the factor in nanoseconds per operation; that disagreement is Open item 12, with its owner and falsifier `[decision]`.

A target with no recorded factor — an emulated-only target, whose measured time is meaningless (025b-10) — stamps the not-calibrated value, as 025b-12 and 013-25 ask, never a made-up value `[decision]`. The value is all ones, the manifest's convention for none (Manifest, Manifest layout), so a reader that overlooked it would compute a time beyond every slice period and refuse rather than accept; 022b-09 calls the same case absent (C-129), and this section encodes it once, as not calibrated `[decision]`.

### Commissioning window section

Section type code 4 `[decision]`. Every module stamps the declared commissioning window, with its expiry time, while the window is open (C-107, C-153); the commissioning tool's announcement and close messages, which open and close it, are laid out by 004-29 `[decision]`:

| Offset (bytes) | Width (bytes) | Type | Meaning | Valid range |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 0 | 8 | u64 | Window identifier: the open window's identifier as the tool's announcement declares it (004-29), the reference that commissioning results carry (C-178) | any 64-bit value, copied from the announcement |
| 8 | 8 | u64 | Expiry time: the window's expiry time as the announcement carries it (C-153), copied verbatim; its unit and clock are those 004-29 states | any 64-bit value, copied from the announcement |

The maximum section size, which is also its fixed size, is 58 + 16 = 74 bytes `[decision]`. One window at a time: a stamp carries at most one commissioning window section, because no landed text lets two windows be open at once; 004-29 decides how a module treats a second announcement while a window is open (004-29, Work 3), and Open item 11, with its owner and falsifier, records it `[decision]`.

The expiry time's unit and clock are fixed by no landed text: C-153 gives only "an expiry time", and 004-29, which depends on this ticket, owns the announcement's field with its width, unit and clock (004-29, Work 1) `[decision]`. This section fixes the stamped width at 64 bits and copies the announced value, so 004-29 states the unit and the clock once for both, and the window identifier is carried the same way; Open item 11 holds both widths with its owner and falsifier `[decision]`. The module removes the section when the window closes, at expiry or on explicit close (C-153); when it reads the expiry is 004-29's receiver rule and epic 005's state machine (C-228) `[decision]`.

### Injections section

Section type code 5 `[decision]`. The section lists the sender's active injections, those of the public injection API and the test-only hooks alike, each stamped like any injection (C-120, C-145): a forced module id and nonce, a stream forced onto a given timeline epoch, a raw packet sent on a media or control socket, and a panic injected into a data, clock or housekeeping thread `[decision]`:

| Offset (bytes) | Width (bytes) | Type | Meaning | Valid range |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 0 | 1 | u8 | Entry count: how many injection entries follow; 0 = no injection active | 0 to 119 |
| 1 | 10 × max 119 | byte blob of injection entries | Injection entry: one active injection, laid out as the entry table below; entries in key order | entry count entries, at most 119 (1 190 bytes) |

The injection entry is 10 bytes; offsets are byte distances from the entry's first byte `[decision]`:

| Offset (bytes) | Width (bytes) | Type | Meaning | Valid range |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 0 | 1 | u8 | Kind: the injection kind, from the kind table below | 1 to 10 |
| 1 | 1 | u8 | Target: for kind 9 the socket the raw packet was sent on, 1 = media, 2 = control; for kind 10 the thread the panic was injected into, 1 = data, 2 = clock, 3 = housekeeping; 0 for every other kind | 1 or 2 for kind 9; 1 to 3 for kind 10; 0 otherwise |
| 2 | 2 | u16 | Stream number (C-10): for kind 8 the stream forced onto an epoch; for kinds 1 to 6 the stream the injection applies to, or 65 535 when it applies to the whole module; 65 535 for kinds 7, 9 and 10 | 0 to 255 (C-09) or 65 535, as the meaning states per kind |
| 4 | 2 | u16 | Timeline epoch (C-10): for kind 8 the epoch the stream is forced onto; 0 for every other kind | any 16-bit value for kind 8; 0 otherwise |
| 6 | 4 | u32 | Uses: for kinds 7 to 10, how many times the hook has been used since the instance started; cumulative, wraps modulo 2^32; 0 for kinds 1 to 6 | any 32-bit value for kinds 7 to 10; 0 otherwise |

The ten kinds are a closed enumeration `[decision]`:

| Kind | Injection | Source |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | clock offset | public injection API (01-architecture.md § 11.2) |
| 2 | clock drift | public injection API (01-architecture.md § 11.2) |
| 3 | packet loss | public injection API (01-architecture.md § 11.2) |
| 4 | packet delay | public injection API (01-architecture.md § 11.2) |
| 5 | reordering | public injection API (01-architecture.md § 11.2) |
| 6 | per-hop delay | public injection API (01-architecture.md § 11.2) |
| 7 | forced module id and nonce | test-only hook (C-120) |
| 8 | stream forced onto a given timeline epoch | test-only hook (C-120) |
| 9 | raw packet sent on a media or control socket | test-only hook (C-120) |
| 10 | panic injected into a data, clock or housekeeping thread | test-only hook (C-145) |

Sizes `[decision]`: 119 × 10 = 1 190 bytes of entries, a body of 1 + 1 190 = 1 191 bytes, and the maximum section size is 58 + 1 191 = 1 249 bytes, within the control bound (C-17). At most 119 entries per section, because ⌊1 193 / 10⌋ = 119 `[decision]`. No constant bounds the injections active at once, so the type's total maximum is chosen as one section's worth, 119, and at its maxima the type needs ⌈119 / 119⌉ = 1 section: a reader finds every active injection in the one section, and a hook used 1 000 times, as 017b-07 asks, is one entry whose uses are 1 000 `[decision]`. The bound's owner and falsifier are in Open item 15 `[decision]`.

The kinds are the ten 013-09 names, the six of the public injection API (01-architecture.md § 11.2) and the four test-only hooks (C-120, C-145) `[decision]`. The enumeration is closed: a kind outside 1 to 10 drops the section (Validity rules of the section types), and an eleventh kind arrives only with a major version or as a new section type, which a v1 receiver skips by its length, because a receiver that ignored an entry it cannot read would miss an injection, and an injected run must never pass as a real one (013-09) `[decision]`. The key order is kind, then target, then stream number, each ascending, and a key appears once `[decision]`.

The forced module id and nonce are not carried in the entry: a module whose identity the hook forced runs under it, so its stamp's sender module id and instance nonce (Stamp section) and its hellos carry the forced values, and the kind 7 entry records that they were forced `[decision]`. An entry of kinds 1 to 6 is present while the injection is active; an entry of kinds 7 to 10 is present from the hook's first use until the instance ends, because each use is stamped (015b-12, 015b-14) and nothing in the design ends the injected state of an instance that a hook has touched (013-09: an injected run can never pass as a real one) `[decision]`. The parameters of the public kinds — an offset, a drift rate, a loss pattern — have no landed encoding and are not carried, nor are the per-call details 017b-07 asks for; Open item 15, with its owner and falsifier, records both `[decision]`.

### Capability states section

Section type code 6 `[decision]`. The section reports each capability of the sender in one of the four states of C-129, the closed enumeration every capability entry, the receive-timestamp section and the health section's observed DSCP use `[decision]`:

| Offset (bytes) | Width (bytes) | Type | Meaning | Valid range |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 0 | 1 | u8 | Entry count: how many capability entries follow | 9 to 255 |
| 1 | 2 × max 255 | byte blob of capability entries | Capability entry: one capability and its state, laid out as the entry table below; entries in ascending capability id | entry count entries, at most 255 (510 bytes) |

The capability entry is 2 bytes; offsets are byte distances from the entry's first byte `[decision]`:

| Offset (bytes) | Width (bytes) | Type | Meaning | Valid range |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 0 | 1 | u8 | Capability id, from the capability table below; the key, each id at most once | 1 to 255; 1 to 9 in v1, 10 to 255 unused and accept-any (Reserved fields) |
| 1 | 1 | u8 | State of the capability, from the state table below | 1 to 4 |

The capability states, a closed enumeration of exactly the four values of C-129, at the byte values 013-01 gives them `[decision]`:

| Value | State | Meaning |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | present | the capability is granted, or holds by construction |
| 2 | absent | the capability was requested and is not available |
| 3 | unverified | the capability may hold, and the module cannot confirm it |
| 4 | not requested | a deliberate configuration choice, as distinct from a denial (C-129) |

The capabilities of v1 `[decision]`:

| Id | Capability | Sources |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | real-time scheduling granted on the data threads and the clock thread | C-127, C-147 |
| 2 | memory locked | C-127, C-147 |
| 3 | CPU idle states restricted, best-effort | C-127 |
| 4 | control isolation: housekeeping cannot delay the data path | C-161 |
| 5 | drop accounting: the module counts the drops of its receiving sockets and interface | 01-architecture.md § 11.1; 013-06, 022a-17, 026b-08, 029-17 |
| 6 | DSCP marking: the module's sends carry the DSCP classes of C-13 | 01-architecture.md § 11.1; C-13; 013-06, 022a-17, 026b-08 |
| 7 | fault injection: the public injection API or the test-only hooks are built in | 01-architecture.md § 11.1, § 11.2; C-120, C-145; 013-06, 026b-17, 026b-08 |
| 8 | holder-eligible (holder eligible): the module may stand as a candidate for clock holder, the eligibility of C-113 and C-213 | 01-architecture.md § 11.1; Hello, Capability bits; 013-06, 026b-08 |
| 9 | bounded real-time: the data threads and the clock thread run with bounded scheduling latency, hard real-time | 01-architecture.md § 10.1; 022a-17 |

Sizes `[decision]`: 255 × 2 = 510 bytes of entries, a body of 1 + 510 = 511 bytes, and the maximum section size is 58 + 511 = 569 bytes. The capability id is one byte and each id appears once, so the type's total maximum is 255 entries, and at its maxima it needs ⌈255 / 255⌉ = 1 section; a v1 sender writes exactly the nine entries of ids 1 to 9, a body of 1 + 18 = 19 bytes `[decision]`.

Ids 1 to 4 are the capabilities the constants name `[decision]`: strict mode requires real-time scheduling on the data and clock threads and locked memory, and restricting CPU idle states is best-effort and stamped (C-127); control isolation is C-161's. Real-time scheduling is one capability, as 013-01 and 01-architecture.md § 11.1 have it: present only when granted on every data thread and on the clock thread, absent when denied on any of them, otherwise unverified when one of them cannot be confirmed, and not requested when the configuration asks for it on none `[decision]`. The states are reported, never acted on here: strict mode refuses an unverified capability, treating it as absent (C-128), which is behaviour `[decision]`.

Ids 5 to 8 are the four further capabilities 01-architecture.md § 11.1 lists, which no constant names and which landed tickets need in the stamp `[decision]`: 013-06 AC 1 round-trips "all capability fields (source plus 9 states)"; 022a-17 AC 1 asks for "literal values *absent* for drop accounting"; 026b-17 AC 5 for "The stamp's `fault_injection` field is `Present`" in a build with the hook; 026b-08 AC 2 justifies a vector as not applicable by "holder_eligible = false, or a capability stamped absent or unverified"; and 029-17 AC 2 renders "drop accounting: unverified" `[decision]`. What each reports, from those texts `[decision]`:

- **Drop accounting (5).** Whether the module can count the datagrams dropped at its receiving sockets and interface, the per-socket overflow and interface drop counters of the Drops group (01-architecture.md § 11.1) and of the receiver report's socket and interface drop fields (Receiver report, Fixed part): absent where the platform gives no such counter, as 022a-17 stamps it on Windows; 01-architecture.md § 10.1 lists overflow accounting as report-only on macOS and Windows `[decision]`.
- **DSCP marking (6).** Whether the module's sockets mark its sends with the classes of C-13, sync CS6, media EF and control CS5: absent when marking is refused, as 022a-17 item 7 has it ("absent (marking refused)"); whether the marking survives the path is read from the observed DSCP of the per-connection health section and from 004-28's sections, not from this capability `[decision]`.
