- **Fault injection (7).** Whether the public injection API (01-architecture.md § 11.2) or the test-only hooks (C-120, C-145) are built in: present in any build that contains the hook, as 026b-17 asks, and absent where injection is not built, which 01-architecture.md § 11.2 allows on embedded ("It may be absent on embedded (stamped)"); the injections section lists the injections active, this capability whether any can be `[decision]`.
- **Holder-eligible (8).** Whether the module may stand as a candidate for clock holder, the eligibility C-113 and C-213 read, which the hello carries as bit 0 of its capability bits (Hello, Capability bits) `[decision]`. The two state one fact: the stamp's state is present exactly when the hello's bit 0 is 1, and otherwise absent (the module cannot be holder, as embedded, macOS and Windows nodes in v1, Hello, Capability bits) or not requested (configured not to stand); the hello's bit is the authority the election reads (Election messages, Holder announcement), and a reader that finds the two different follows the hello `[decision]`.

**Bounded real-time (9).** 022a-17 AC 1 asks for "literal values *absent* for drop accounting and bounded real-time", the same kind of evidence that requires ids 5 to 8, so the capability has an id now `[decision]`. Its meaning comes from 01-architecture.md § 10.1, whose capability table gives Windows real-time threads as "MMCSS Pro Audio, not hard real-time" and lists "bounded real-time" among what is report-only on Windows: bounded real-time is present when the data threads and the clock thread run with a bounded scheduling latency, hard real-time, as distinct from id 1, which says only that real-time scheduling was granted `[decision]`. What each platform reports, as far as the design text says `[decision]`: Windows stamps it absent (§ 10.1, "not hard real-time"; 022a-17 item 9, "bounded real-time *absent*"); a device stamps it present by construction, the § 10.1 entry for embedded real-time threads being "By construction" (C-147 gives real-time scheduling and memory lock by construction on a device); for Linux (`SCHED_FIFO`, needs privilege) and macOS (time-constraint policy plus workgroup) the design text does not say, which Open item 13 records with its owner and falsifier `[decision]`.

What a sender writes is bound by the constants, not checked by a receiver `[decision]`: strict mode is hosted-only, and a device stamps real-time scheduling and memory lock as present by construction (C-147); control isolation is present on a hosted platform by construction of the thread model, and absent or unverified on a device unless the implementer shows a bound (C-161). A receiver accepts every state for every id, because the state is the sender's claim about itself `[decision]`.

**Closed states, open ids.** The state enumeration is closed: 0 and 5 to 255 drop the section (Validity rules of the section types), and a fifth state would change what an existing field means to a v1 receiver, so it arrives only with a major version (Versioning, Minor and major changes), or as a new section type with its own layout, which a v1 receiver skips by its length (Section type codes) `[decision]`. The capability id is accept-any from 10 to 255: a later ticket stamps a further capability as a minor change by assigning it an id in this table, and a v1 receiver ignores an entry whose id it does not know (Reserved fields, Fields of the regime stamp section types); a capability a ticket names that has no id here yet is Open item 13, with its owner and falsifier `[decision]`.

### Receive-timestamp source section

Section type code 7 `[decision]`. The section reports where the sender's receive timestamps come from, one of the four sources of C-131, with one of the four capability states of C-129 (Capability states section): a per-module capability the slice header leaves to the stamp (Slice header, Receive timestamps) `[decision]`:

| Offset (bytes) | Width (bytes) | Type | Meaning | Valid range |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 0 | 1 | u8 | Source: the receive-timestamp source, from the source table below | 1 to 4 |
| 1 | 1 | u8 | State: the C-129 capability state of that source, from the state table of the capability states section | 1 to 4 |

The receive-timestamp sources, a closed enumeration of exactly the four values of C-131 `[decision]`:

| Value | Source | Meaning |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | kernel | hosted OS kernel stamp |
| 2 | userspace | clock read after receive |
| 3 | mac-hardware | Ethernet MAC timestamp register, embedded |
| 4 | interrupt | stamped in the receive interrupt, embedded |

The maximum section size, which is also its fixed size, is 58 + 2 = 60 bytes, and every stamp carries the section once `[decision]`. One source per module, the per-module capability of Slice header, Receive timestamps; which source each platform has and in which state is task 001's and epic 013's to state (013-01, 013-22), not a wire rule `[decision]`. The source enumeration is closed as the states are: 0 and 5 to 255 drop the section, and a fifth source arrives only with a major version or a new section type `[decision]`.

### Section count at the maxima

Each type at its maxima, from the tables above `[decision]`:

| Type | Code | Maximum section size | Entries per section | Total maximum | Sections at the maxima |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| multi-packet regime | 1 | 1 251 bytes | 149 | 256 streams (C-09) | 2 |
| per-connection health | 2 | 1 229 bytes | 18 | 32 256 member entries (C-09, C-217) | 1 792 |
| calibration factor | 3 | 66 bytes | fixed | one value | 1 |
| commissioning window | 4 | 74 bytes | fixed | one window | 1 |
| injections | 5 | 1 249 bytes | 119 | 119 injections, chosen | 1 |
| capability states | 6 | 569 bytes | 255 | 255 capabilities | 1 |
| receive-timestamp source | 7 | 60 bytes | fixed | one source | 1 |

At their maxima the seven types need 2 + 1 792 + 1 + 1 + 1 + 1 + 1 = 1 799 sections `[decision]`.

**Reported: the 64-section bound is exceeded at the maxima, and the design owner's decision.** At their maxima the seven types need more than 64 sections, 1 799 against 64, an excess of 1 799 − 64 = 1 735 sections, which meets the falsifier of Open item 1 (the section types at their maxima need more than 64 sections): the bound of Open item 1 is falsified for a module at the maxima of C-09 and C-217, and this was reported to the design owner on 2026-11-16 `[decision]`. Under merged sender rule 7, "a stamp that needs more cannot be sent": above the bound a module sends no stamp at all, so peers and tools receive none of its sections, neither its capability states nor its injections, which contradicts C-18 for that module (multicast on any regime change, refreshed every 10 s) and 013-09's "an injected run can never pass as a real one" `[decision]`. The threshold below which a stamp still fits is (64 − 7) × 18 = 1 026 member entries: the 7 sections the six other types need at their maxima are taken from the 64, and every section that 004-28's section types, or a later per-member type, need lowers the threshold by 18 entries, one health section's worth `[decision]`. The design owner decided on 2026-11-16 to cap the connections one module may hold by a constant that the design owner sets in the constants table, sized so that the per-connection health section always fits the 64-section stamp and enforced at admission by mechanisms that exist: on the consumer side the refusal InputCount, "the receiver's input count would be exceeded, a full connection table included (012-13)" (Admission messages, Refusal reasons), and on the source side the local outcome of 012-12, where `request` "returns `Err(RequestError::TableFull)` and sends nothing"; there is no wire change `[decision]`. The condition the constant must satisfy, stated rather than valued: the member entries a module can hold at the cap, as producer and as consumer together and ended connections it still lists included, are at most the threshold once 004-28's sections are counted, (64 − 7 − s) × 18 for the s sections 004-28's types need at that cap. In connections, as arithmetic and not as the constant's value: a connection has up to 32 members (C-08), one entry each, so 1 026 entries hold ⌊1 026 / 32⌋ = 32 connections of 32 members, 32 × 32 = 1 024 entries, before 004-28's sections are counted; until the constant is in the constants table, the falsification stands `[decision]`. Status, 004-28: the alignment group's sections grow with the same consumer-side entries as the health sections, so s depends on the cap itself, and Section count with the types of 004-28 restates the condition in a self-consistent closed form, with its arithmetic `[decision]`.

No choice of widths avoids the excess `[decision]`: an entry that carries the eleven counters at even one byte each, with one byte to name its member, is at least 12 bytes, so at most ⌊1 193 / 12⌋ = 99 entries fit a section and the health type needs at least ⌈32 256 / 99⌉ = 326 sections; with this layout the producer side alone needs ⌈16 128 / 18⌉ = 896 sections `[decision]`.

What fits `[decision]`: the six other types need 2 + 1 + 1 + 1 + 1 + 1 = 7 sections at their maxima, which leaves 64 − 7 = 57 sections for the health type and for the types of 004-28. At 013-13's test size of 256 member entries the health type needs ⌈256 / 18⌉ = 15 sections, so 64 − 7 − 15 = 42 remain for 004-28's types `[decision]`. Codes 8 to 15 remain for later types of this block and 16 to 31 for 004-28; the type codes leave 004-28 its block, and the section count leaves it at most 57 sections at the maxima of these types other than health `[decision]`.

### Validity rules of the section types

A section of one of these types that passes the stamp's validity rules (Validity rules above) is checked against its type's rules in the order of the table below, the first rule that applies deciding; a section dropped under them is counted once and leaves its stamp incomplete, as Validity rules above states, and the last row is decided when every section of the stamp version is held, before the stamp completes `[decision]`. Every outcome names a member of the closed Rejection reasons enumeration whose Layers cell lists the control layer, and the enumeration is unchanged `[decision]`. The BadLength outcomes for a value outside a closed enumeration or a fixed range are provisional, as for section type 0, and are confirmed by 004-21 (Open item 16, with its owner and falsifier) `[decision]`:

| Invalid form | Required outcome |
|---|---|
| section length inconsistent with the type: under 1 for a list type, other than 1 + entry count × entry size for a list type (8 bytes per multi-packet entry, 65 per health entry, 10 per injection entry, 2 per capability entry), or other than the fixed body of a fixed type (8 bytes for the calibration factor, 16 for the commissioning window, 2 for the receive-timestamp source) | drop the section and count under BadLength: the body does not fill the size the type declares `[decision]` |
| entry count above its maximum (149 multi-packet, 18 health, 119 injections, 255 capability states), or an entry count of 0 in a multi-packet or health section, or below 9 in a capability states section | drop the section and count under BadLength: a count outside its fixed range `[decision]` |
| unknown enumeration value: a capability state, an observed-DSCP state or a receive-timestamp state of 0 or 5 to 255; a receive-timestamp source of 0 or 5 to 255; an injection kind of 0 or 11 to 255 | drop the section and count under BadLength `[decision]` — provisional: the closed enumerations of C-129, C-131 and 013-09 have no other value in v1 |
| reserved bits non-zero: bits [7:1] of byte 18 or bits [7:6] of byte 64 of a health entry | drop the section and count under ReservedNonZero (Reserved fields, General rule for later tickets) |
| value outside its fixed range: a stream number above 255 (65 535 allowed only where the injection entry states it), packets per slice below 2, frames per slice of 0, a calibration factor of 0, an observed DSCP other than 0 with a state other than present, or an injection entry's target, stream number, epoch or uses other than its kind allows | drop the section and count under BadLength `[decision]` — provisional, as above |
| entries out of key order, or one key twice, within one section: multi-packet entries by stream number, health entries by role, peer module id, stream number and admission request id, injection entries by kind, target and stream number, capability entries by capability id | drop the section and count under BadLength: the entries do not form the declared list `[decision]` |
| a capability states section that lacks an entry for one of the capability ids 1 to 9 | drop the section and count under BadLength `[decision]` — provisional, as above |
| a stamp version, all its sections held, whose sections of one type break the type's shape: entries out of key order or a key repeated across the type's sections read in section index order; none, or more than one, section of a type every stamp carries once (calibration factor, injections, capability states, receive-timestamp source); more than one commissioning window section | the version does not complete: drop every section held for that stamp version, keep the complete stamp held before it, and count once under BadLength: the sections do not form one declared shape `[decision]` |

Not a rejection: an entry of a capability id from 10 to 255 is checked for its state and its order and otherwise ignored, because the id is accept-any (Reserved fields, Fields of the regime stamp section types); a section of type code 8 to 15 is a section of an unknown type, skipped by its length (receiver rule 8) `[decision]`. No other value of these types is a malformed case: a module id, a session, a window identifier, an expiry time, a timeline epoch outside an injection entry and every counter accept every value their types hold `[decision]`.

### Observed DSCP of peers section

Section type code 16 `[decision]`. The section carries the peer verification of sync and control DSCP of C-152: every module reports, per peer, the DSCP observed on that peer's hellos, and the holder also reports, per follower, the DSCP observed on that follower's sync requests and the count of its sync requests dropped above the accepted rate of 200 per second (C-204, C-112) `[decision]`. The classes senders request are control CS5 (40) for hellos and sync CS6 (48) for sync requests (C-13); the section reports what arrived, so a peer reads from it whether a switch or a host re-marked its traffic (011-25, 022a-21) `[decision]`. A connection's media DSCP is not here: the per-connection health section carries it (C-47), and this section carries the two classes C-152 adds (013-26) `[decision]`:

| Offset (bytes) | Width (bytes) | Type | Meaning | Valid range |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 0 | 1 | u8 | Entry count: how many peer entries follow | 1 to 63 |
| 1 | 14 × max 63 | byte blob of peer entries | Peer entry: one peer of the sender's discovery map, laid out as the entry table below; entries in ascending peer module id | entry count entries, at most 63 (882 bytes) |

The peer entry is 14 bytes; offsets are byte distances from the entry's first byte `[decision]`:

| Offset (bytes) | Width (bytes) | Type | Meaning | Valid range |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 0 | 8 | u64 | Peer module id (C-11): the module id of the peer, as its hellos carry it; the key, each peer at most once in the stamp | any 64-bit value |
| 8 | 1 | u8 | Hello DSCP code: the DSCP of the IPv4 header of the last hello received from this peer, or a not observed code (DSCP codes below) | 0 to 63: the DSCP observed; 130, 131 or 132: not observed, with the C-129 state absent, unverified or not requested; every other value drops the section |
| 9 | 1 | u8 | Sync DSCP code, holder only: in a holder's stamp, the DSCP of the IPv4 header of the last sync request of this follower that the holder answered (C-188), or a not observed code; 128 in the stamp of a module that is not the holder (DSCP codes below) | 0 to 63: the DSCP observed; 130, 131 or 132: not observed, with the C-129 state absent, unverified or not requested; 128: no sync observation, the sender is not the holder; every other value drops the section |
| 10 | 4 | u32 | Dropped sync requests, holder only: requests of this follower dropped because they exceeded 200 per second (C-204, C-112), counted in requests; cumulative from 0 when the entry first carries a sync observation, wraps modulo 2^32 | any 32-bit value in a holder's stamp (wrap rule below); 0 when the sync DSCP code is 128 |

Sizes `[decision]`: 63 × 14 = 882 bytes of entries, a body of 1 + 882 = 883 bytes, and the maximum section size is 58 + 883 = 941 bytes, within the control bound of 1 252 bytes (C-17). A module has at most 63 peers, because a medianet has at most 64 active modules (C-34) and a module holds no entry for itself (C-217), and a holder has at most 63 real followers (C-114), all of them among its peers; the type's total maximum is therefore 63 entries, one per peer, and at its maxima it needs ⌈63 / 63⌉ = 1 section `[decision]`. One section holds them all: ⌊1 193 / 14⌋ = 85 entries would fit, and the entry count stops at the type's total of 63, so the type is never split and a stamp version carries at most one section of it `[decision]`.

Which stamp carries it `[decision]`: a module's stamp carries the section exactly while its discovery map holds at least one peer, with one entry per peer the map holds (C-217), so the entry count is never 0. Entries are in ascending peer module id, each module id once: sorting keeps the bytes of an unchanged state identical from one send to the next, which sender rule 3 needs to repeat a version (Section types of the constants table) `[decision]`.

**DSCP codes.** Each observation is one byte `[decision]`. A code from 0 to 63 is the DSCP observed, the C-129 state present. A code above 63 is not observed: 130, 131 and 132 carry the C-129 state of the observation, 128 plus the state's value (Capability states section) — absent (2), the sender cannot read the DSCP of received packets; unverified (3), no hello, or for a follower no answered request, has been read yet; not requested (4), a configuration that turns the observation off, which no landed configuration does, so a v1 sender does not write it and a receiver accepts it, as the health section does — and 128 is no sync observation, written only in the sync code of a module that is not the holder `[decision]`. The not observed codes lie above 63, so no value read from a packet is mistaken for one, and unverified reads distinct from 0, as 013-26 asks (AC 3) and as 026b-12 asks when the device cannot read the received byte ("each entry carries that C-129 state", AC 3) `[decision]`. One byte rather than the health entry's state byte beside a 6-bit DSCP (Per-connection health section), because of the case the health entry never has: a module that is not the holder has no sync observation at all, and C-129 has no state for it — absent says the observation was requested and is not available, not requested says a configuration turned it off — so the health convention would need a fifth state value outside C-129's closed enumeration, or a separate flag, while one byte holds the 64 DSCP values, the three non-present states and the no-observation case `[decision]`. Size is not the reason: the health convention fits one section too, a 16-byte entry, 63 × 16 = 1 008 bytes of entries and a section of 58 + 1 009 = 1 067 bytes; the cost of this choice is that a reader decodes the observed DSCP of a peer and the observed DSCP of a connection (Per-connection health section) by two conventions `[decision]`. Codes 64 to 127 are a DSCP above 63, and 129 and 133 to 255 are no code; each drops the section (invalid forms below) `[decision]`.

**Holder only.** In a holder's stamp every entry carries a sync observation: the holder cannot know which peers will follow it before their requests arrive, so a peer whose request it has not answered yet reads unverified (131), as 015a-10 has a follower not yet heard read "unverified" (AC 6) `[decision]`. In the stamp of a module that is not the holder every sync code is 128 and every dropped count 0, which is how a reader tells no value from a count of 0, as 013-27 asks (AC 2: "its reader reports it absent, distinct from 0"), and how 022a-21's edge, never the holder, "publishes no sync-request DSCP observations" (AC 4) `[decision]`. The sync DSCP is that of a request the holder answered, because a request dropped above the accepted rate is dropped "before any work is done" (C-204) and its DSCP is not recorded `[decision]`. A section that mixes 128 in some entries with sync observations in others states a holder and a non-holder at once; this section does not decide whether that is a malformed form, treats it as a fault of the sender and not a rejection, and hands the question to 004-21 as Open item 19, with its owner and falsifier `[decision]`.

**Which follower a request comes from.** The sync request carries no follower identity, and the Sync request and reply section leaves to this section how the per-follower entries of C-152 and C-204 name a follower (Sync request and reply, Exchange rules) `[decision]`. The entry names a follower by its module id, the key every reader of the stamp knows a peer by, so a follower's sync observation and dropped count sit in the same entry as its hello observation (013-27: "it goes in the same entry") `[decision]`. The holder attributes a request to a follower before decoding it, by the datagram's source (015a-23: "The follower is identified from the receive metadata (`RxMeta.from`) before the request is decoded"), and binds that source to the module id of a peer of its discovery map; no field carries that binding, the hello carries no sync port, and two modules on one host share an address (Admission messages, Common admission fields; Receiver report, Bundle identity and member rows), so the binding is not a wire rule and is Open item 18, with its owner and falsifier `[decision]`. A request the holder cannot bind to a peer is counted in no entry `[decision]`.

**Wrap rule.** The dropped count is 32 bits and wraps modulo 2^32, as every counter of the health section and of the receiver report does `[decision]`. It starts at 0 when the entry first carries a sync observation — when the sender becomes the holder, or when a peer enters its discovery map while it holds — and a later entry for a peer whose entry left the stamp starts at 0 again `[decision]`. A holder change restarts every count: a module that stops holding writes 128 and 0 in every entry, and a module that becomes the holder, again or for the first time, starts each count at 0, so a reader that sees an entry's sync code go from 128 to an observation takes the count as a new baseline, never as a change `[decision]`. A reader takes the change between two stamps of one sender instance (module id and instance nonce) as the difference modulo 2^32, read as a signed value, the health section's rule (Per-connection health section, Wrap rule), when the entry carries a sync observation in both and in every version between them that the reader holds; otherwise it takes the later value as a new baseline, and a reader that missed the version in which the entry was absent cannot tell that restart from a change `[decision]`. In one refresh period of 10 s (C-18) fewer than 1 488 000 000 packets of any kind reach a module at 100 Gbit/s, the bound the health section derives from a frame size recalled from IEEE Std 802.3 `[memory]`, inside the 2 147 483 647 that a 32-bit signed difference resolves, so a reader of two consecutive refreshes reads every change `[decision]`.

The two observations and the dropped count move during a run, so under sender rule 3 (Sender rules) a refresh in which one of them moved is sent under a new stamp version, as for the health counters `[decision]`. 013-26 and 013-27 flag these values as counters, which do not change epic 013's StampId (013-05); this layout neither decides nor carries that flag `[decision]`.

A section of this type that passes the stamp's validity rules is checked against the rows below in order, the first that applies deciding, as Validity rules of the section types does for 004-25's types; every outcome names a member of the closed Rejection reasons enumeration whose Layers cell lists the control layer, the enumeration is unchanged, and the BadLength outcomes for a code outside the closed set are provisional, as for 004-25's types (Open item 16, with its owner and falsifier) `[decision]`:

| Invalid form | Required outcome |
|---|---|
| section length inconsistent with the type: under 1, or other than 1 + entry count × 14 | drop the section and count under BadLength: the body does not fill the size the type declares `[decision]` |
| entry count above its maximum of 63 (C-34, C-217), or an entry count of 0 | drop the section and count under BadLength: a count outside its fixed range `[decision]` |
| a DSCP value above 63: a hello or sync DSCP code from 64 to 127 | drop the section and count under BadLength `[decision]` — provisional: a DSCP is six bits wide, so no packet carries a value above 63 |
| a DSCP code outside the closed set: 129 or 133 to 255 in either code, or 128 in a hello DSCP code | drop the section and count under BadLength `[decision]` — provisional, as above |
| a dropped sync request counter other than 0 in an entry whose sync DSCP code is 128 | drop the section and count under BadLength `[decision]` — provisional: a module that is not the holder drops no sync request |
| entries out of ascending peer module id, or one peer module id twice, within one section | drop the section and count under BadLength: the entries do not form the declared list `[decision]` |
| a stamp version, all its sections held, with more than one section of this type | the version does not complete: drop every section held for that stamp version, keep the complete stamp held before it, and count once under BadLength: the sections do not form one declared shape `[decision]` |

No value of a peer module id, and no value of a dropped count beside a sync observation, is a malformed case `[decision]`.

### Alignment group section

Section type code 17 `[decision]`. The section carries the alignment group of C-157, C-160 and C-182: per aligned input of the sender, the lateness percentiles 50th, 99th and 99.9th and the maximum, over a rolling 60 s window (C-160, C-182), the maximum since the connection went live (C-182) and the reclaimable latency, offset − (current maximum lateness + headroom) when positive (C-157) `[decision]`. Lateness is origin-to-arrival lateness, the arrival time of a slice minus T(p), the network time of its position, which a consumer measures per input and which includes every upstream hop (01-architecture.md § 4.3; 009-13) `[decision]`:

| Offset (bytes) | Width (bytes) | Type | Meaning | Valid range |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 0 | 1 | u8 | Entry count: how many input entries follow in this section | 1 to 18 |
| 1 | 63 × max 18 | byte blob of input entries | Input entry: one aligned input of the sender, laid out as the entry table below; entries in key order | entry count entries, at most 18 (1 134 bytes) |

The input entry is 63 bytes: 15 that name the input as the health section names its member, and six 64-bit figures; offsets are byte distances from the entry's first byte `[decision]`:

| Offset (bytes) | Width (bytes) | Type | Meaning | Valid range |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 0 | 8 | u64 | Peer module id (C-11): the module id of the input's source, as in the consumer entry of the per-connection health section | any 64-bit value |
| 8 | 2 | u16 | Stream number (C-10) of the input's member stream at its source | 0 to 255 (C-09) |
| 10 | 4 | u32 | Admission request id: the request id of the admission handshake that admitted the input's connection (Admission messages, Common admission fields), as in the consumer entry of the per-connection health section | any 32-bit value |
| 14 | 1 bit | uint | Role, bit [0:0] of byte 14: 1 = consumer, the sender consumes the stream, as in the consumer entry of the per-connection health section; an aligned input is a consumer-side member | 1 only |
| 14 | 7 bits | uint | Reserved, bits [7:1] of byte 14 (Reserved fields, General rule for later tickets) | 0 only |
| 15 | 8 | i64 | 50th percentile of the input's lateness over the rolling 60 s window (C-160, C-182), in nanoseconds | any 64-bit signed value; −2^63 = no lateness sample in the window (order rule below) |
| 23 | 8 | i64 | 99th percentile of the input's lateness over the rolling 60 s window (C-160, C-182), in nanoseconds | as the first percentile; at least it (order rule below) |
| 31 | 8 | i64 | 99.9th percentile of the input's lateness over the rolling 60 s window (C-160, C-182), in nanoseconds | as the first percentile; at least the figure before it (order rule below) |
| 39 | 8 | i64 | Maximum of the input's lateness over the rolling 60 s window (C-160, C-182), exact, in nanoseconds | as the first percentile; at least the figure before it (order rule below) |
| 47 | 8 | i64 | Maximum of the input's lateness since its connection went live (C-182), kept across a re-alignment and restarted only by a new admission (C-229), in nanoseconds | any 64-bit signed value; −2^63 = no live sample yet, only while the four window figures are also −2^63; at least the window maximum (order rule below) |
| 55 | 8 | i64 | Reclaimable latency (C-157): offset − (the input's maximum over the rolling 60 s window + the C-52 headroom of that maximum) when positive, in nanoseconds; 0 = not positive | 0 = none; a positive value is offset − (maximum + headroom), and the offset is at most A (C-53, C-96); a negative value drops the section |

Sizes `[decision]`: 18 × 63 = 1 134 bytes of entries, a body of 1 + 1 134 = 1 135 bytes, and the maximum section size is 58 + 1 135 = 1 193 bytes, within the control bound of 1 252 bytes (C-17). At most 18 entries per section, because ⌊1 193 / 63⌋ = 18, the 1 193 bytes being what a body of at most 1 194 bytes leaves after its one-byte entry count `[decision]`.

**What an aligned input is, and how many there are.** An aligned input is one member stream of one consumer-side connection of the sender: 01-architecture.md § 4.2 has "A fan-in consumer holds each aligned input in a ring indexed by position", 009-13 states "All aligned inputs, including all bundle members, share the consumer's one offset", and 009-04 pins each input as one source address and stream, `pin(&mut self, addr: Addr, stream: StreamId, input: InputIndex)` `[decision]`. An alignment entry therefore corresponds to one consumer entry of the per-connection health section, role 1, and never to a producer entry, and to at most one, because a consumer module holds at most one admitted connection per bundle of a source and a stream belongs to one bundle (Per-connection health section) `[decision]`. No constant bounds a module's aligned inputs below its consumer-side members: C-157, C-160 and C-182 fix what is reported and not how many inputs report it, and C-96 sizes each ring to A, not the number of rings `[decision]`. The type's total maximum is therefore the health section's consumer-side maximum, 63 × 256 = 16 128 entries, the 256 streams of a source (C-09) from each of 63 peers (C-217), and at its maxima the type needs ⌈16 128 / 18⌉ = 896 sections (C-09, C-217), beyond the bound of 64 sections (Section count with the types of 004-28) `[decision]`.

**Which inputs are listed, and the key.** An entry is listed for every aligned input of the sender from the moment its connection first goes live, at the end of aligning (C-50; 009-13), until the connection ends; a re-alignment (C-186, C-215) keeps it listed, as it keeps the maximum since live (C-229), and an input still aligning for the first time has no figure yet `[decision]`. An unaligned input has no entry, since it "has no ring, no lateness sample, no ratchet participation" (009-18), and neither has a producer-side member; the section is carried while at least one entry is listed `[decision]`. The entry names its input by exactly the fields that name a member entry of the health section — peer module id, stream number, admission request id and role (Per-connection health section) — at the same widths, so a reader joins an alignment entry to the health entry with the same key, the consumer entry of the same member; the session is not part of that key and is not repeated `[decision]`. Every alignment entry has that consumer entry in the same stamp version; one that has none is a fault of the sender, not a parse rejection, because deciding it needs the health sections of the whole version, as for the frames per slice of the multi-packet regime section `[decision]`.

**Order and split.** Entries are in the health section's key order restricted to role 1 — peer module id, then stream number, then admission request id, each ascending — with each key once in the stamp `[decision]`. Where the entries do not fit one section, the type is split over several sections of code 17 by the rule of Section types of the constants table: each section is a complete body with its own entry count, and read in section index order the entries of all the type's sections are in key order `[decision]`.

**Reported contradiction: lateness figures per aligned input against one summary per consumer.** Ticket 004-28 asks, in its Work 2, for the alignment group "per input, an input identifier consistent with the health section of 004-25", and 026b-12, in its Work 1, "Per admitted input, take core's lateness percentiles (C-160) and reclaimable latency (C-157)"; 009-25 keeps "Per consumer, a rolling 60 s window (C-182) kept as 60 one-second histograms in a fixed ring, fed by `observe_lateness` from every aligned input", and 009-13's `rolling_max_ns(&self)` and `max_since_live_ns(&self)` take no input. The two cannot both hold: one gives each aligned input its own figures, the other one set of figures per consumer `[decision]`. This section follows its ticket, one entry per aligned input; the swarm cannot edit tickets, the lead reported the contradiction to the design owner on 2026-11-16, and Open item 17 keeps it, with its owner and falsifier `[decision]`.

**Widths and units.** Every figure is a signed 64-bit count of nanoseconds of network time, the unit of the receiver report's current A (Receiver report, Current A), and the type the producing code uses: 009-13's `observe_lateness` takes `lateness_ns: i64`, and 009-25's `AlignmentSummary` holds `p50_ns`, `p99_ns`, `p999_ns`, `max_ns`, `reclaimable_ns` and `max_since_live_ns` as `i64` `[decision]`. The width is not the 32 unsigned bits of the current A: lateness is not bounded by A — an input that stays late with the offset at its ceiling is Demoted (C-109, C-226), and the cannot-meet-A alarm fires on a maximum lateness that, with D and the headroom, exceeds A (C-174) — and it is signed in the producing code, so 32 unsigned bits would need a clamping rule no landed text gives; 64 bits carry the producing value unchanged, and no figure wraps or saturates `[decision]`. The cost is the entry size: with 32-bit figures an entry would be 15 + 24 = 39 bytes and ⌊1 193 / 39⌋ = 30 entries would fit a section, against 18 `[decision]`.

**Order rule and the empty window.** The five lateness figures of an entry are in non-decreasing order, 50th ≤ 99th ≤ 99.9th ≤ maximum ≤ maximum since live, and the order holds for the no-sample value −2^63 too, the least i64 value, which is at most every value and equal to itself, so no state is exempt `[decision]`. The percentiles and the maximum rank the same window's samples, and the window lies within the live period the maximum since live covers (C-160, C-182, C-229) `[decision]`. A sender writes each percentile as at most the window's maximum: 009-25 reports a percentile as the upper edge of its histogram bucket, "so it never under-reports", and its AC 2 has a single sample of 3 ms report all four figures "within one bucket (max exact)", so a bucket edge can lie above the exact maximum; the sender writes the smaller of the two, which still never under-reports, because the exact percentile is at most the exact maximum `[decision]`. The value −2^63 is far outside any lateness a consumer measures; with it the reclaimable latency is 0, since C-157's current maximum lateness does not exist `[decision]`. Both choices, and whether the figures are per input at all, are Open item 17, with its owner and falsifier `[decision]`.

**The states of an entry, from go-live to the end of its connection** `[decision]`:
- **Just live, no sample yet.** 009-13 has the maximum since live start "when `finish_aligning` switches to `Live`", so at go-live it holds no live sample, and an input that falls silent right after go-live never gets one. The entry is listed at once, with all five lateness figures −2^63 and the reclaimable latency 0, and the order rule holds, the five being equal: listed only from its first live sample, a silent live input would have no entry at all, and a reader could not tell it from an input still aligning, while the health section lists the consumer entry from admission and C-182 reports the maximum since live "beside" the window figures for every live connection `[decision]`.
- **Samples in the window.** Every figure is a measured value in nanoseconds, the five in order, and the reclaimable latency is 0 or positive (C-157) `[decision]`.
- **Window empty after silence.** No sample in the last 60 s: the four window figures are −2^63 and the reclaimable latency 0, while the maximum since live keeps the value it reached; the order rule holds, −2^63 being at most that value `[decision]`.
- **Re-alignment** (C-186, C-215). The entry stays listed and the maximum since live is kept: C-229 has "a re-alignment does not reset it. Only a new admission of the connection does", because lateness is measured against the timeline, not against the offset; for the same reason the window figures carry on, and the reclaimable latency is taken against the new offset. 009-13 starts the maximum since live "when `finish_aligning` switches to `Live`", which a re-alignment repeats; this layout follows C-229, as Open item 17 records with its owner and falsifier `[decision]`.
- **A new admission.** A new admission request id makes a new key and a new entry, which starts again at just live, and the entry of the ended connection leaves the section when that connection ends `[decision]`.

**Reclaimable latency.** C-157 defines it as offset − (current maximum lateness + headroom), when positive; per input, this layout takes the current maximum lateness as the input's maximum over the rolling 60 s window (C-182) and the headroom as C-52's for that maximum, the largest of 25 % of it, one slice period and twice the published clock bound, against the consumer's one offset, which every aligned input shares (009-13) `[decision]`. The value 0 means not positive, the "none" 013-08 asks for (AC 6) and the "absent or zero" of 026b-12 (AC 2): a positive reclaimable latency is at least 1 ns, so 0 is never a value C-157 reports `[decision]`. The offset is bounded by A, the ratchet's ceiling (C-53), to which the rings are sized (C-96), and a sink's by A − D (C-226) `[decision]`. The offset itself, A, the required E and a sink's certification state are the regime fields of 013-08's alignment section (Work 2), which no ticket of epic 004 lays out, so this section does not add them; the container lets a later ticket add that type as a minor change, with a code from 32 to 65 535 (Section type codes) `[decision]`. Their owner is epic 013, ticket 013-08, which lays them out in its own regime section, apart from the counter section of its Work 3 that this type is, the case the README of epic 004 gives under gap R8 to "Each epic that stamps state, as a minor change"; falsifier: epic 013 finds that its regime section needs a layout or a code from epic 004, or a reader needs one of those fields from a section of this ticket `[decision]`.

The figures move as samples enter and leave the window, so under sender rule 3 (Sender rules) a refresh in which any of them moved is sent under a new stamp version `[decision]`. 013-08 puts these figures in a section it flags as counters, which do not change epic 013's StampId (013-05); this layout neither decides nor carries that flag `[decision]`. While a commissioning window is open the figures are unqualified production measurements (C-178), which a reader tells from the commissioning window section the same stamp carries (Section types of the constants table) `[decision]`.

A section of this type that passes the stamp's validity rules is checked against the rows below in order, the first that applies deciding, as for the observed DSCP of peers; every outcome names a member of the closed Rejection reasons enumeration whose Layers cell lists the control layer, the enumeration is unchanged, and the BadLength outcomes for a value outside its fixed range are provisional, as for 004-25's types (Open item 16, with its owner and falsifier) `[decision]`:

| Invalid form | Required outcome |
|---|---|
| section length inconsistent with the type: under 1, or other than 1 + entry count × 63 | drop the section and count under BadLength: the body does not fill the size the type declares `[decision]` |
| entry count above its maximum of 18, or an entry count of 0 | drop the section and count under BadLength: a count outside its fixed range `[decision]` |
| reserved bits non-zero: bits [7:1] of byte 14 of an input entry | drop the section and count under ReservedNonZero (Reserved fields, General rule for later tickets) |
| a stream number above 255 (C-09), or a role bit of 0: a producer-side member, which has no alignment entry | drop the section and count under BadLength `[decision]` — provisional: a value outside its fixed range |
| percentiles out of non-decreasing order: an entry whose 50th, 99th and 99.9th percentiles, window maximum and maximum since live do not satisfy 50th ≤ 99th ≤ 99.9th ≤ maximum ≤ maximum since live (C-182) | drop the section and count under BadLength `[decision]` — provisional: the figures do not form the declared order |
| an empty-window value of −2^63 in some but not all four window figures, a negative reclaimable latency, or a reclaimable latency other than 0 while the window figures hold the empty-window value | drop the section and count under BadLength `[decision]` — provisional: a value outside its fixed range |
| a maximum since live holding its no-sample value while a window figure holds a measured value | drop the section and count under BadLength `[decision]` — provisional: a window sample is a live sample, so the maximum since live has one too |
| entries out of key order, or one key twice, within one section | drop the section and count under BadLength: the entries do not form the declared list `[decision]` |
| a stamp version, all its sections held, whose input entries are out of key order or repeat a key across the type's sections read in section index order | the version does not complete: drop every section held for that stamp version, keep the complete stamp held before it, and count once under BadLength: the sections do not form one declared shape `[decision]` |

No value of a peer module id or an admission request id is a malformed case, and a lateness figure accepts every value the order rule allows `[decision]`. The reclaimable latency is not checked against the offset or A, which this section does not carry: a value above A is a fault of the sender, which a reader holding 013-08's regime fields can tell `[decision]`.

### Section count with the types of 004-28

Each type of 004-28 at its maxima, from the tables above `[decision]`:

| Type | Code | Maximum section size | Entries per section | Total maximum | Sections at the maxima |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| observed DSCP of peers | 16 | 941 bytes | 63 | 63 peers (C-34, C-217) | 1 |
| alignment group | 17 | 1 193 bytes | 18 | 16 128 aligned inputs (C-09, C-217) | 896 |

At their maxima the two types need s = 1 + 896 = 897 sections `[decision]`. With the seven types of 004-25 a stamp at the maxima of C-09 and C-217 needs 1 799 + 897 = 2 696 sections; leaving the health type aside, the six other types of 004-25 and the two types of 004-28 need 7 + 897 = 904 sections `[decision]`. Codes 18 to 31 stay unassigned in v1, and a section of one of them is a section of an unknown type, skipped by its length (receiver rule 8) `[decision]`.

**Reported: the 64-section bound is exceeded further by 004-28's types, and the cap the design owner decided.** At their maxima the six other types of 004-25 and the two types of 004-28 need 904 sections against the bound of 64 before a single health section is counted, an excess of 904 − 64 = 840 sections, and with the health type the stamp needs 2 696 sections, an excess of 2 696 − 64 = 2 632; this meets again, for 004-28's types, the falsifier of Open item 1 (the section types at their maxima need more than 64 sections), whose owner is epic 004 with this ticket, and it is reported to the design owner with this ticket on 2026-11-16 `[decision]`. Under merged sender rule 7, "a stamp that needs more cannot be sent": above the bound a module sends no stamp at all, so peers and tools receive none of its sections, its peer DSCP observations and alignment figures included, which contradicts C-18 for that module `[decision]`. The threshold below which the health entries still fit is (64 − 7 − s) × 18 member entries for the s sections 004-28's types need; at the maxima s is 897 and the threshold is (64 − 7 − 897) × 18 = −15 120 entries, negative: the alignment group alone needs more than the 57 sections the six other types leave, and no health entry fits `[decision]`. The design owner decided on 2026-11-16 to cap the connections one module may hold by a constant the design owner sets in the constants table, enforced at admission with no wire change (Section count at the maxima); at that cap s is 1 plus the alignment sections of the aligned inputs the cap allows, so s grows with the cap, and the condition the constant must satisfy is restated below in closed form `[decision]`.

**The condition on the cap, with 004-28's types.** A module with p producer-side and c consumer-side member entries, a of them aligned inputs with a listed entry (a ≤ c), and at least one peer needs 7 sections for the six other types of 004-25, 1 for the observed DSCP of peers, ⌈(p + c) / 18⌉ for the health type and ⌈a / 18⌉ for the alignment group, so, under the assumptions stated below, its stamp fits exactly when ⌈(p + c) / 18⌉ + ⌈a / 18⌉ ≤ 56, the merged threshold p + c ≤ (64 − 7 − s) × 18 with s = 1 + ⌈a / 18⌉ `[decision]`. The cap bounds connections, not their kind, so the condition must hold at the worst mix the cap allows: for a fixed number of member entries e the count is largest when every entry is consumer-side and aligned, a = c = e, so the constant must satisfy 2 × ⌈e / 18⌉ ≤ 56, that is ⌈e / 18⌉ ≤ 28 and e ≤ 28 × 18 = 504, for e the most member entries the cap lets a module hold, ended connections it still lists included `[decision]`.

Worked examples, as arithmetic and not as the constant's value `[decision]`: with no producer-side entries, the largest c that fits is 504, which needs ⌈504 / 18⌉ = 28 health sections and 28 alignment sections, and 7 + 1 + 28 + 28 = 64; at c = 505 the module needs ⌈505 / 18⌉ = 29 sections of each type, and 7 + 1 + 29 + 29 = 66, over the bound. Read as connections of 32 members (C-08), one entry per member, 504 entries hold ⌊504 / 32⌋ = 15 connections, 15 × 32 = 480 entries, which need ⌈480 / 18⌉ = 27 sections of each type, and 7 + 1 + 27 + 27 = 62; a 16th connection makes 16 × 32 = 512 entries, ⌈512 / 18⌉ = 29 sections of each type, and 7 + 1 + 29 + 29 = 66. Without aligned inputs the alignment group needs no section and 56 × 18 = 1 008 entries fit, ⌊1 008 / 32⌋ = 31 connections of 32 members, one fewer than the 32 of Section count at the maxima, because the observed DSCP of peers takes one section `[decision]`.

**What the condition assumes.** The condition and its 504 entries rest on three things, and each moves the cap `[decision]`. First, it assumes the figure width this section decides, six i64 figures of 8 bytes, hence a 63-byte entry and 18 entries per section, giving ⌈e / 18⌉ + ⌈e / 18⌉ ≤ 56 and e ≤ 28 × 18 = 504 member entries, 15 connections of 32 members (Widths and units) `[decision]`. With 32-bit figures the entry would be 15 + 24 = 39 bytes and ⌊1 193 / 39⌋ = 30 entries would fit a section, the condition would be ⌈e / 18⌉ + ⌈e / 30⌉ ≤ 56, and the largest e would be 35 × 18 = 630, since 630 needs 35 + 21 = 56 sections and 631 needs 36 + 22 = 58, which is ⌊630 / 32⌋ = 19 connections of 32 members, 19 × 32 = 608 entries; with only the reclaimable latency at 32 bits the entry would be 59 bytes, ⌊1 193 / 59⌋ = 20 entries per section, and the largest e would be 29 × 18 = 522, since 522 needs 29 + 27 = 56 sections and 523 needs 30 + 27 = 57, which is 16 connections of 32 members `[decision]`. The 64-bit width is this section's decision, and its cost at the cap is 630 − 504 = 126 member entries, four connections of 32 members, the price of carrying the producing value unclamped `[decision]`. Second, it assumes per-input alignment entries, one per aligned input, as the ticket asks, while 009-25 and 009-13 compute one summary per consumer (Open item 17, with its owner and falsifier): if one section held all of a module's per-consumer summaries, the condition would be ⌈e / 18⌉ ≤ 64 − 7 − 1 − 1 = 55, so e ≤ 55 × 18 = 990, 30 connections of 32 members, 30 × 32 = 960 entries; how many sections such summaries take depends on a per-consumer layout that does not exist, so 990 holds only under that one-section assumption `[decision]`. Third, it counts no section type that is not laid out yet, and every section such a type needs lowers the bound by 18 member entries: the path MTU of each connection, which belongs in each side's regime stamp (Path-MTU probe pair, Exchange; Section type codes); 013-08's regime alignment section, with the offset, A, the required E and the certification state; and 006-19's 16-bit timeline epoch per record set `[decision]`. A per-connection path-MTU type grows with the connections themselves, so its sections enter the condition as the alignment group's do, and the cap the design owner sets must leave room for it `[decision]`.

Until the constant is in the constants table and meets this condition, the falsification of Open item 1 stands, with its owner and falsifier as that item states `[decision]`.

### Open items

**Open item 1 — the maximum section count.** A stamp has at most 64 sections, so at most 80 128 bytes; the bound is chosen, not derived from a constant, because no constant bounds the connections a stamp reports and the section types are laid out after this ticket; a stamp that needs more sections cannot be sent. Owner: epic 004 (this ticket), with 004-25 and 004-28, which fix the maximum counts of their section types and show that each section fits, and 013-13 (stamp size budget and splitting rule), which computes the worst-case stamp. Falsifier: the section types at their maxima, or a module's real connection count, need more than 64 sections `[decision]`. Status, 2026-11-16: this falsifier is met at the maxima of C-09 and C-217 (Section count at the maxima), and it was reported to the design owner, who decided the same day to cap the connections one module may hold by a constant enforced at admission, with no wire change; owner of that constant: the design owner `[decision]`.

**Open item 2 — which stamps a module holds, and a small device's memory.** A receiver needs at most 160 256 bytes per peer, 10 096 128 bytes for 63 peers (C-217), which may exceed a small device's memory; a module need not hold every peer's stamp — admission reads the stamps of the modules it admits (005-23), a collector reads every module's (013-17) — but no ticket says which stamps a device must hold. Owner: ticket 005-23 (regime stamp coupling), which names what the control machines read from a peer's stamp, with 013-17 (stamp collector) and epic 026, the device side as 013-24 names it. Falsifier: a device must hold the stamps of more peers than its memory holds at 160 256 bytes each, the device-memory case C-34's falsifier names for the peer map `[decision]`.

**Open item 3 — the stamp version and the StampId.** Epic 013 leaves to epic 004 with epic 005 whether the hello's version is the StampId and whether a counter refresh bumps it (README of epic 013, Remaining gaps, item 1; 013-05, 013-24). This section answers: the version is a counter, not the StampId, which epic 013 derives from regime fields only; a refresh whose counters moved is sent under a new version, because one version must name one set of section bytes for the repeat and disagreement rules to hold; the hello names the version last multicast; the fetch load this causes under the landed discovery texts is Open item 6. Owner: epic 013 (013-05 StampId derivation, 013-24 publication schedule, whose Work 5 exposes a change notification for the hello, and 013-17 stamp collector), with 005-23, which must agree for epic 005. Falsifier: a reader needs the version to change only on a regime change, for example to tell a regime change from a counter refresh without the StampId `[decision]`.

**Open item 4 — the framing epic 013 proposes.** Beyond the independent stamp-format version, reported above as a contradiction (Section type codes), 013-02 proposes a total length and a section version per section, skipping a known section of a newer section version, and 013-13 proposes splitting a large section into parts carrying the StampId, the section type, a part index and a part count, all marked pending epic 004. This layout has none of them: a new layout of a section type takes a new code, the stamp's own section index and count give completeness for every type at once, and a type spanning several sections is several sections of that type. Owner: tickets 013-02 (stamp envelope and section framing) and 013-13 (stamp size budget and splitting rule), with 013-22 (stamp specification document). Falsifier: epic 013 must change the layout of a section type within a major version while keeping its code, or a reader of a type spanning several sections cannot tell that type is whole from the stamp's section index and count `[decision]`.

**Open item 5 — what a receiver uses while a newer version is incomplete.** The receiver keeps the last complete stamp, which the hello's rule calls stale, until the newer version completes, and never uses an incomplete one; the design does not say whether a reader — admission reading E and the calibration factor (005-23), the collector recording counters (013-17) — may act on a stale copy. Owner: ticket 005-23 (regime stamp coupling), with 013-17 (stamp collector). Falsifier: a reader must not act on a stale copy, so it has no stamp of the peer during every reassembly, or an admission decided on a stale copy differs from the one the newer stamp gives `[decision]`.

**Open item 6 — the fetch rule, the fetch load and the answer load.** Present disagreement: the epic 013 file names the risk of this question as "Peers fetch on every refresh, or miss a regime change"; 011-23 raises `StampVersionChanged`, "which the stamp consumer uses to issue the unicast fetch", on every change of the version a hello carries; and 005-07 says "a hello with a newer stamp version leads to a unicast stamp request". Under sender rule 3 every refresh with moved counters is a new version, so those texts make every module fetch every peer's stamp every 10 s: 63 × 63 = 3 969 fetches per 10 s at most, and 3 969 × 80 128 = 318 028 032 bytes of answers per 10 s. Receiver rule 9 binds instead: a receiver fetches only when its held copy is stale and the multicast of that version did not complete; how long it waits before fetching and how often it asks again are fixed by no constant. The answers are unbounded too: at a cold start up to 63 peers fetch one module's stamp, 63 × 80 128 = 5 048 064 bytes from one responder, a cold-starting requester receives up to 63 × 80 128 = 5 048 064 bytes of answers from its 63 peers, and an answer is up to 80 128 / 40 = 2 003.2 times its 40-byte request, unauthenticated in v1 (C-115), with no rule bounding how many requests a responder answers. Owner: tickets 011-23 (regime stamp version tracking) and 005-07 (discovery map transition table), which must adopt rule 9, with 005-23 (regime stamp coupling) for the wait, 013-24 (publication schedule, which emits the answers) and epic 015 (sending, C-144) for an answer rate. Falsifier: once 011-23 and 005-07 follow rule 9, a receiver that lost no section still fetches; or at a cold start the answers delay a module's hellos beyond their 1 s period (C-30); or a receiver stays stale longer than one refresh period of 10 s because its requests or their answers are lost `[decision]`.

**Open item 7 — fetching a stamp by its id.** 013-17 sends a unicast request when a record references a StampId the collector has not seen; the request names a module, and the answer is that module's current stamp, not a stamp of a given id. Owner: ticket 013-17 (stamp collector). Falsifier: the collector must resolve an id of a stamp the module no longer publishes `[decision]`.

**Open item 8 — stamps before the first hello and during a quarantine.** A section of a module with no discovery entry, of a module id in quarantine after a collision (C-46; 005-06: third parties "ignore both"), or of an instance nonce other than the latest hello's, is ignored, so a receiver takes a stamp only from the instance the discovery map holds, and none while the id is quarantined. Owner: tickets 005-06 and 005-07 (discovery map states and transitions), with 013-17 (stamp collector). Falsifier: a receiver, a collector for example, must take a stamp from a module before its first hello, or must keep a quarantined id's stamps during the 3 s quarantine (C-46, C-31) `[decision]`.

**Open item 9 — provisional BadLength outcomes.** An unused or unexpected message type, a request naming another module and a section of type 0 are counted under BadLength provisionally, as in the Hello and Admission messages sections. Owner: ticket 004-21 (validity rules completeness audit). Falsifier: 004-21 finds a member of the closed enumeration that names one of these cases better, or rules that one of them is not a rejection `[decision]`.

**Open item 10 — a device's immediate multicast on any field change.** 026a-11 has a device multicast its stamp "on every version change" (Work 3), and its AC 2 reads "changing any stamp field bumps the version number in the next hello and triggers an immediate multicast"; its Work 2 (a bump on any field change) and Work 4 (an answer "unicast to the requester") agree with this section, but once the device stamps per-connection counters (026b-11), AC 2 multicasts on every counter change, while sender rule 2 and 013-24 hold a counter change for the next 10 s refresh. Owner: epic 026, tickets 026a-11 (stamp publication over network) and 026b-11 (per-connection counters), with 013-24. Falsifier: a device must multicast a counter change before the next refresh, for example because a reader needs a counter fresher than 10 s `[decision]`.

**Open item 11 — the commissioning window's identifier, expiry and count.** The commissioning window section carries the window identifier and the expiry time at 64 bits each, copied from the announcement, whose unit and clock 004-29 states, and at most one window at a time; no landed text fixes the expiry's unit or clock (C-153 gives only "an expiry time"), nor whether two windows may be open at once. Owner: ticket 004-29 (commissioning window announcement and close messages), with epic 005 for the window's state machine (C-228). Falsifier: 004-29 lays out a window identifier or an expiry time wider than 64 bits; or it lays the expiry out as a duration relative to the announcement, which a verbatim copy in the stamp misstates once time has passed since the announcement; or it lets a second window be open at one module while the first is open `[decision]`. Status, 2026-11-16: decided by Commissioning window messages (004-29), and the falsifier does not hold: the window identifier and the expiry time are 64 bits each in the announcement, the expiry an absolute instant in nanoseconds of network time, from 0 to 2^63 − 1, so the verbatim copy stays true however late it is stamped; and an announcement of another window is ignored while a window is open (Commissioning window messages, Receiver rules, rule 5), so one module never holds two windows at once `[decision]`.

**Open item 12 — the calibration factor's unit.** The factor is a u64 of femtoseconds per operation, all ones meaning not calibrated. Present disagreement: 012-03 holds the factor in nanoseconds per operation for its integer check `cost_bound_ops × ns_per_op ≤ remaining CPU ns per slice`, and whole nanoseconds truncate a factor below 1 ns per operation to 0, which admits any cost; and 022b-09 AC 2 reads "A machine without a recorded factor stamps the factor *absent* (C-129)", which this layout encodes once, as the not-calibrated value. Owner: ticket 012-03 (receiver capacity checks), with 005-23 (regime stamp coupling), 025b-10 (cost measurement and calibration) and 022b-09 (Windows cost calibration factor). Falsifier: 012-03 keeps whole nanoseconds per operation and a factor recorded by 025b-10 lies below 1 ns per operation; or 025b-10 records a factor that needs a resolution finer than 1 fs per operation; or 022b-09 needs a stamped state other than not calibrated for a machine without a factor `[decision]`.

**Open item 13 — capabilities beyond ids 1 to 9, and bounded real-time on Linux and macOS.** The capability states section stamps nine capabilities: the four the constants name, the four further ones 01-architecture.md § 11.1 lists, and bounded real-time from § 10.1, each needed by landed tickets (Capability states section); ids 10 to 255 are accept-any, so a later ticket adds a capability as a minor change. 026a-04 declares two more device capability fields, `dont_fragment` and `rx_dscp_observation` ("needed for the observed-DSCP counter, C-47"), and sends any field 013's stamp object lacks to "013's extension rule or record it as an open item with owner epic 013"; its `iface_drop_counters` is drop accounting, id 5. Separately, the design text does not say which state bounded real-time takes on Linux or macOS. Owner: epic 013 (013-22 stamp specification document, whose field table gives each field's source per profile, and 013-01), with 026a-04 for the device fields and the platform epics for bounded real-time (016, the Linux real-time setup backend; 021, the macOS backend). Falsifier: epic 013 maps `dont_fragment` or `rx_dscp_observation` to a stamp field while no id from 10 is assigned here for it; or 013-22's per-profile table gives Linux or macOS a bounded real-time source that none of the four C-129 states can express `[decision]`.

**Open item 14 — the per-connection records of 013-23 and 012-23.** 013-23 sketches dead as a flag, eight counters as u64 and the producer side writing no observed DSCP; 012-23 likewise has `dead: bool`, u64 counters and "a terminal slot stays listed until reused"; 029-09 records the epic's open question of where the connection state and the Demoted health state are published. This layout counts entries into Dead as the receiver report does, carries all eleven counters at 32 bits with a wrap rule, has the producer stamp the DSCP its consumer reports, as C-47 and the receiver report have it, and keys an entry by role, peer module id, stream number and admission request id, so a terminal slot and its re-admission are two entries; it publishes no connection state. Owner: tickets 013-23 (per-connection summary section) and 012-23 (per-connection stamp record), with 005-15 (connection states) and 029-09 (connection states and gap classes) for the state question. Falsifier: a reader must know from one stamp entry alone whether a connection is dead now, which neither the dead count nor the hello's Dead alarm tells; or a reader misses refreshes for long enough that a 32-bit counter wraps between the two stamps it compares; or the source side reuses the request id of an ended connection with the same consumer for the same stream while 012-23 still lists the ended one, so one key appears twice `[decision]`.

**Open item 15 — what the injections section leaves out, and its uses counter.** At most 119 active injections, one section; the parameters of the six public kinds have no landed encoding and are not carried, and the per-call record 017b-07 asks for (destination, length and a hash of each raw packet) is reduced to a use count per kind and socket. The uses field is a counter inside a section that 013-09 flags as regime ("Platform and Injection are regime fields") and that 013-05 derives the StampId from, so under that flag every use of a hook would change the StampId and start a new record set, where a use count is a counter. Owner: ticket 013-09 (platform, drops and injection sections), with 013-05 (StampId derivation) for the regime or counter flag of the uses field, 017b-07 (raw packet test hook) and 015b-12 (test-only injection hooks). Falsifier: a scenario needs more than 119 distinct active injections in one module; or a reader must recover an injection's parameters, or a raw packet's destination, length or hash, from the stamp rather than from the test log; or, once 013-05 and 013-09 are implemented, a run that uses one hook repeatedly yields one record set per use `[decision]`.

**Open item 16 — provisional BadLength outcomes of the section types.** A value outside a closed enumeration or a fixed range of the section types, and a capability states section missing one of the ids 1 to 9, are counted under BadLength provisionally, as section type 0 is (Open item 9). Owner: ticket 004-21 (validity rules completeness audit). Falsifier: 004-21 finds a member of the closed enumeration that names one of these cases better, or rules that one of them is not a rejection `[decision]`.

**Open item 17 — what the alignment figures are computed over.** Present disagreement: ticket 004-28 (Work 2, "per input") and 026b-12 (Work 1, "Per admitted input") stamp the figures per aligned input, while 009-25 keeps "Per consumer, a rolling 60 s window (C-182) kept as 60 one-second histograms in a fixed ring, fed by `observe_lateness` from every aligned input", and its `alignment_summary`, like 009-13's `rolling_max_ns`, `max_since_live_ns` and `reclaimable_ns`, returns one value per consumer; the Alignment group section follows its ticket, one entry per input, with the reclaimable latency of each input taken against the consumer's one offset. Two further points meet 009-25: a percentile reported as the upper edge of its bucket can exceed the exact maximum, which the sender writes as the smaller of the two; and no landed text says what an empty window yields, which the section encodes as −2^63 in the four window figures with a reclaimable latency of 0. 018-24 also resets a sink's maximum since live "when the connection goes live again", which after a re-alignment (C-186) contradicts C-229, which keeps it until a new admission, and 009-13 starts it "when `finish_aligning` switches to `Live`", which a re-alignment repeats; the section follows C-229. The per-input against per-consumer contradiction is reported to the design owner (Alignment group section, Reported contradiction), and this item keeps it until the design owner rules. Owner: ticket 009-25 (alignment group stamp figures), with 009-13 (offset estimator), 013-08 (clock and alignment sections) and 018-24 (sink lateness statistics and cannot-meet-A). Falsifier: 009-25 lands with per-consumer figures only, so a sender can fill an input's entry only with its consumer's figures; or 009-25 or 013-08 fixes a value for an empty window other than none; or 018-24 or 009-13 lands resetting the maximum since live at a re-alignment `[decision]`.

**Open item 19 — a section that states a holder and a non-holder at once.** In the observed DSCP of peers section a module that is not the holder writes 128 in every sync code, and a holder writes a sync observation in every entry; a section that mixes 128 in some entries with sync observations in others is treated as a fault of the sender, not a rejection, because no rule of the type decides it from the section's bytes alone. Owner: ticket 004-21 (validity rules completeness audit), with 013-26 (per-peer observed DSCP entries) for the reader. Falsifier: 004-21 rules that the mixed section is a malformed form with an outcome, or a reader of 013-26 must reject it to stay correct, for example because it takes a mixed section's dropped counts as a holder's `[decision]`.

**Open item 18 — which follower a sync request comes from.** The observed DSCP of peers section names a follower by its module id, and the holder attributes each sync request to a follower by the datagram's source before decoding it (015a-23, `RxMeta.from`); the request carries no identity (Sync request and reply, Exchange rules), the hello carries no sync port, and two modules on one host share an address (Admission messages, Common admission fields; Receiver report, Bundle identity and member rows), so no wire rule binds a request's source address and port to one module id. Owner: ticket 015a-23 (holder sync request rate cap), with 015a-10 (clock thread) and epic 005's discovery map (005-06, 005-07), which holds each peer's address. Falsifier: with two followers on one host, the holder counts one follower's dropped requests, or records its sync DSCP, in the other's entry or in no entry `[decision]`.

## Commissioning window messages

The commissioning window is how the commissioning tool declares when it may drive test traffic: outside a declared window that every module stamps the tool is read-only (C-107); the tool announces the window on the control group with an expiry time, modules stamp it while it is open, and it closes at expiry or on explicit close (C-153) `[decision]`. This section gives the two messages codes 48 and 49 of the block the Hello section reserves for 004-29, lays out the window announcement and the window close, states their sizes, the receiver rules for the open, expiry and explicit-close transitions and for every other announcement or close a module can receive, and every invalid form with its outcome `[decision]`. Epic 004 owns the field layout and epic 005 the state machine that holds the window (C-228). The stamped form of the open window is the commissioning window section of the regime stamp (Regime stamp, Commissioning window section), laid out by 004-25, which this section fills and does not change: this section adds no regime stamp section type, so the section count and the condition on the connection cap (Regime stamp, Section count with the types of 004-28) are unchanged `[decision]`.

Behaviour the layouts serve, restated as fact: both messages are control messages, sent by the commissioning tool by UDP over IPv4 (C-100), multicast to the control group with TTL 1, at DSCP CS5 (40) (C-13), and both start with the common control header and its C-12 authentication reservation at the v1 values of C-115, scheme 0 and tag 128 zero bits (Hello, Common control header) `[decision]`. A message of either kind that arrives by unicast is parsed by the same rules, as a hello is (Hello) `[decision]`. The announcement and the close are, with stamp requests, the only messages the tool sends outside a window (C-107; 028b-09, AC 4) `[decision]`.

The tool is not a module: it sends no hello, has no entry in any discovery map and has no data plane, so its message counter cannot be a data-plane liveness counter (C-115; Hello, Liveness counter) `[decision]`. The tool adds one to its counter for each control message it sends, starting from any value; receivers accept any value and no receiver reads it, so two sends of one message may differ in bytes 8 to 15, and every comparison of two announcements below runs from offset 32 to the end, as the regime stamp compares its sections (Regime stamp, Sender rules, rule 6) `[decision]`.

### Window announcement

The window announcement, code 48 (Hello, Control message type codes), opens a window, or repeats or extends the window it names. Little-endian (Byte order), packed with no padding (Alignment and padding); bytes 0 to 31 are the common control header (Hello, Common control header) `[decision]`:

| Offset (bytes) | Width (bytes) | Type | Meaning | Valid range |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 0 | 2 | u16 | Magic 0x4d4e, the ASCII letters M and N, fixed by 004-04 | 0x4d4e only |
| 2 | 2 | u16 | Protocol version, major in the high byte (bits 15..8), minor in the low byte; v1 packs 1.0 as 0x0100 (004-04) | major 1, any minor (Versioning, Receiver behaviour); senders write 0x0100 |
| 4 | 2 | u16 | Domain id (C-11); 0 is the unconfigured domain | 1 to 65535; 0 = unconfigured, ignored and counted (C-173) |
| 6 | 1 | u8 | Message type: 48 = commissioning window announcement (Control message type codes) | 48 only |
| 7 | 1 | u8 | Authentication scheme (C-12); 0 = none in v1 (C-115) | 0 only |
| 8 | 8 | u64 | Message counter (C-12): live (C-115); the tool adds one per control message it sends (above) | any 64-bit value |
| 16 | 16 | u128 | Authentication tag (C-12): 128 zero bits in v1 (C-115) | 0 only |
| 32 | 8 | u64 | Window identifier: drawn by the tool for this window and written unchanged in every announcement and close of it; the reference commissioning results carry (C-178) and the value the commissioning window section stamps (Regime stamp) | 1 to 18 446 744 073 709 551 615 (2^64 − 1); 0 is not a window identifier |
| 40 | 8 | u64 | Tool identity: the identity of the commissioning tool that announces the window, the same in every announcement of the window | any 64-bit value |
| 48 | 8 | u64 | Expiry time: the absolute instant at which the window closes, in nanoseconds of network time (timeline.md, Domain line and position map), one instant for every module | 0 to 9 223 372 036 854 775 807 (2^63 − 1) ns; 2^63 and above is out of range |

The announcement's maximum size, which is also its fixed size, is 56 bytes: 32 + 8 + 8 + 8 = 56, tiling bytes 0 to 55 with no gap, far within the 1 252-byte control bound (C-17) `[decision]`.

**Window identifier.** The identifier is stable: the tool draws it once, when it opens the window, and writes it unchanged in every repeat and every extension of the announcement and in the close; every module stamps it verbatim in its commissioning window section (Regime stamp, Commissioning window section), and every commissioning result taken in the window records it (C-178; 028b-10 records a `window` in each sink and pair record), so one value names the window from its announcement to the records that reference it `[decision]`. It is unique enough without any coordination between tools: the tool draws it uniformly at random from the 2^64 − 1 non-zero values, as a module draws its instance nonce afresh (C-11), so no registry and no counter shared between tools or between invocations of one tool is needed `[decision]`. Among n windows the chance that any two share an identifier is at most n × (n − 1) / 2 / (2^64 − 1); for one million windows that is 499 999 500 000 / 18 446 744 073 709 551 615, about 2.7 × 10^-8, as arithmetic and not as a bound on how many windows a deployment opens `[decision]`. An identifier derived from the tool identity and a counter was not chosen: `window open` and `window close` are separate invocations of the tool (028b-09), so the counter would have to persist between them, while a random draw needs no state, and the close and an extension take the identifier from the stamps that carry it (028b-09, `window status`) `[decision]`. Zero is not a window identifier: a zeroed buffer is then never a valid announcement or close, as code 0 is never a control message (Hello, Control message type codes), and a record or a tool can write 0 for no window without naming a real one `[decision]`. The width is the stamp's, 64 bits (Regime stamp, Commissioning window section) `[decision]`.

**Tool identity.** The commissioning tool is not a module and has no module id: the design gives module ids to modules (C-11), and the tool never takes part in a live data path (028a-01) `[decision]`. The announcement therefore names its tool by a tool identity of its own, 64 bits, the width of the module id and the instance nonce (C-11), every value valid `[decision]`. The tool writes the same value in every announcement of one window, across its invocations, so that a module can tell a repeat or an extension by the announcing tool from an announcement that only reuses the identifier (Receiver rules, rule 5) `[decision]`. Modules do not stamp it — the commissioning window section carries the identifier and the expiry only — and grant nothing by it, because v1 authenticates no control message (C-115); how the tool chooses and keeps the value is commissioning tooling's (Open item 3) `[decision]`.

**Expiry time.** The expiry is an absolute instant, never a span counted from the announcement: every module stamps it verbatim (Regime stamp, Commissioning window section, Open item 11), 028b-09 expects `window status` to show the same expiry at every module (AC 1), and an announcement that arrives late, twice or re-sent then names the same instant at every module, where a span would end the window later at a module that received it later `[decision]`. Its clock is network time, the one time scale the design gives every module that follows the holder: position is network time counted from a domain epoch origin (timeline.md, Domain line and position map), and a module's local monotonic time or wall clock is not shared, so an expiry on either would name different instants at different modules `[decision]`. Its unit is the nanosecond, the unit in which timeline.md counts network time and the epoch origin `origin_ns` (timeline.md, The epoch origin), so the test of 007b-12, the window still stamped 1 ns before the expiry and no longer at it (AC 2), is exact `[decision]`.

Network time is a signed 64-bit nanosecond count (timeline.md, The epoch origin), and the field is a u64, the type of the stamped expiry (Regime stamp, Commissioning window section): its valid range is 0 to 2^63 − 1, the non-negative network times, on which the u64 and the i64 readings of the 8 bytes agree, so the stamp's verbatim copy has one meaning; a value of 2^63 or above names no network time an i64 can hold and is out of range (Validity rules) `[decision]`. 2^63 − 1 ns is about 292 years of network time, so the range bounds no window by its length; no constant bounds how long a window may be, and this section sets no maximum length (Open item 4) `[decision]`. An expiry before network time 0 cannot be written; whether network time can be negative on a v1 holder is not stated by timeline.md, which gives only the i64 range (Open item 5) `[decision]`. The tool computes the expiry as its network time at the announcement plus the length the operator states (028b-09: `window open --duration <d>`, with no default); the design does not say how the tool, which is not a module, reads network time (Open item 3) `[decision]`.

### Window close
